Core Capabilities (The Ways) 
In the National Preparedness Goal (NPG), FEMA has identified 32 areas that are critical to the 
emergency support functions.  These capabilities are referenced in many national preparedness 
efforts, including the National Planning Frameworks. The Goal grouped the capabilities into five 
mission areas, based on where they most logically fit. Some fall into only one mission area, while 
some others apply to several mission areas. 
Response core capabilities describe the grouping of response actions that can be taken to 
stabilize and re-establish the lifelines.  FEMA executes lines of effort (LOE) to operationalize the 
core capabilities for response and recovery planning and operations. 
Core Capabilities Descriptions (Alpha) 
 
ACCESS CONTROL AND IDENTITY VERIFICATION 
 
Mission Area: Protection 
 
Description: Apply and support necessary physical, technological, and cyber measures to 
control admittance to critical locations and systems. 
COMMUNITY RESILIENCE 
 
Mission Area: Mitigation 
 
Description: Enable the recognition, understanding, communication of, and planning for risk 
and empower individuals and communities to make informed risk management decisions 
necessary to adapt to, withstand, and quickly recover from future incidents.  
CRITICAL TRANSPORTATION 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Provide transportation (including infrastructure access and accessible 
transportation services) for response priority objectives, including the evacuation of people 
and animals, and the delivery of vital response personnel, equipment, and services into the 
affected areas. 
CYBERSECURITY 
 
Mission Area: Protection 
 
Description: Protect (and if needed, restore) electronic communications systems, 
information and services from damage, unauthorized use, and exploitation. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
41 
 
ECONOMIC RECOVERY 
 
Mission Area: Recovery 
 
Description: Return economic and business activities (including food and agriculture) to a 
healthy state and develop new business and employment opportunities that result in a 
sustainable and economically viable community. 
ENVIRONMENTAL RESPONSE/HEALTH AND SAFETY 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Conduct appropriate measures to ensure the protection of the health and safety 
of the public and workers, as well as the environment, from all-hazards in support of 
responder operations and the affected communities.  
FATALITY MANAGEMENT SERVICES 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Provide fatality management services, including decedent remains recovery 
and victim identification, working with local, state, tribal, territorial, insular area, and Federal 
authorities to provide mortuary processes, temporary storage or permanent internment 
solutions, sharing information with mass care services for the purpose of reunifying family 
members and caregivers with missing persons/remains, and providing counseling to the 
bereaved.  
FIRE MANAGEMENT AND SUPPRESSION 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Provide structural, wildland, and specialized firefighting capabilities to manage 
and suppress fires of all types, kinds, and complexities while protecting the lives, property, 
and the environment in the affected areas. 
FORENSICS AND ATTRIBUTION 
 
Mission Area: Prevention 
 
Description: Conduct forensic analysis and attribute terrorist acts (including the means and 
methods of terrorism) to their source, to include forensic analysis as well as attribution for an 
attack and for the preparation for an attack in an effort to prevent initial or follow-on acts 
and/or swiftly develop counter-options. 
HEALTH AND SOCIAL SERVICES 
 
Mission Area: Recovery 
 
Description: Restore and improve health and social services capabilities and networks to 
promote the resilience, independence, health (including behavioral health), and well-being of 
the whole community. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
42 
 
HOUSING 
 
Mission Area: Recovery 
 
Description: Implement housing solutions that effectively support the needs of the whole 
community and contribute to its sustainability and resilience. 
INFRASTRUCTURE SYSTEMS 
 
Mission Area: Response, Recovery 
 
Description: Stabilize critical infrastructure functions, minimize health and safety threats, 
and efficiently restore and revitalize systems and services to support a viable, resilient 
community. 
INTELLIGENCE AND INFORMATION SHARING 
 
Mission Areas: Prevention, Protection 
 
Description: Provide timely, accurate, and actionable information resulting from the 
planning, direction, collection, exploitation, processing, analysis, production, dissemination, 
evaluation, and feedback of available information concerning physical or cyber threats to the 
United States, its people, property, or interests; the development, proliferation, or use of 
WMDs; or any other matter bearing on U.S. national or homeland security by Federal, state, 
local, and other stakeholders. Information sharing is the ability to exchange intelligence, 
information, data, or knowledge among Federal, state, local, or private sector entities, as 
appropriate. 
INTERDICTION AND DISRUPTION 
 
Mission Areas: Prevention, Protection 
 
Description: Delay, divert, intercept, halt, apprehend, or secure threats and/or hazards. 
LOGISTICS AND SUPPLY CHAIN MANAGEMENT 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Deliver essential commodities, equipment, and services in support of impacted 
communities and survivors, to include emergency power and fuel support, as well as the 
coordination for access to community staples.  Synchronize logistics capabilities and enable 
the restoration of impacted supply chains. 
LONG-TERM VULNERABILITY REDUCTION 
 
Mission Area: Mitigation 
 
Description: Build and sustain resilient systems, communities, and critical infrastructure and 
key resources lifelines so as to reduce their vulnerability to natural, technological, and human-
caused threats and hazards by lessening the likelihood, severity, and duration of the adverse 
consequences. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
43 
 
MASS CARE SERVICES 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Provide life-sustaining and human services to the affected population to include 
hydration, feeding, sheltering, temporary housing, evacuee support, reunification, and 
distribution of emergency supplies. 
MASS SEARCH AND RESCUE OPERATIONS 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Deliver traditional and atypical search and rescue capabilities, including 
personnel, services, animals, and assets to survivors in need, with the goal of saving the 
greatest number of endangered lives in the shortest time possible. 
NATURAL AND CULTURAL RESOURCES 
 
Mission Area: Recovery 
 
Description: Protect natural and cultural resources and historic properties through 
appropriate planning, mitigation, response, and recovery actions to preserve, conserve, 
rehabilitate, and restore them consistent with post-disaster community priorities and best 
practices and in compliance with applicable environmental and historical preservation laws 
and executive orders. 
ON-SCENE SECURITY AND PROTECTION 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Ensure a safe and secure environment through law enforcement and related 
security and protection operations for people and communities located within affected areas 
and also for response personnel engaged in lifesaving and life-sustaining operations. 
OPERATIONAL COMMUNICATIONS 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Ensure the capacity for timely communications in support of security, 
situational awareness, and operations by any and all means available, among and between 
affected communities in the impact area and all response forces. 
OPERATIONAL COORDINATION 
 
Mission Areas: All 
 
Description: Establish and maintain a unified and coordinated operational structure and 
process that appropriately integrates all critical stakeholders and supports the execution of 
core capabilities. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
44 
 
PHYSICAL PROTECTIVE MEASURES 
 
Mission Area: Protection 
 
Description: Implement and maintain risk-informed countermeasures, and policies 
protecting people, borders, structures, materials, products, and systems associated with key 
operational activities and critical infrastructure sectors. 
PLANNING 
 
Mission Areas: All 
 
Description: Conduct a systematic process engaging the whole community as appropriate in 
the development of executable strategic, operational, and/or community-based approaches 
to meet defined objectives. 
PUBLIC HEALTH AND MEDICAL SERVICES 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Provide lifesaving medical treatment via Emergency Medical Services and 
related operations and avoid additional disease and injury by providing targeted public health 
and medical support and products to all affected population. 
PUBLIC INFORMATION AND WARNING 
 
Mission Areas: All 
 
Description: Deliver coordinated, prompt, reliable, and actionable information to the whole 
community through the use of clear, consistent, accessible, and culturally and linguistically 
appropriate methods to effectively relay information regarding any threat or hazard, as well 
as the actions being taken and the assistance being made available, as appropriate. 
RISK AND DISASTER RESILIENCE ASSESSMENT 
 
Mission Area: Mitigation 
 
Description: Assess risk and disaster resilience so that decision makers, responders, and 
community members can take informed action to reduce their entity's risk and increase their 
resilience. 
RISK MANAGEMENT FOR PROTECTION PROGRAMS AND ACTIVITIES 
 
Mission Area: Protection 
 
Description: Identify, assess, and prioritize risks to inform Protection activities, 
countermeasures and investments. 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
45 
 
SCREENING, SEARCH, AND DETECTION 
 
Mission Areas: Prevention, Protection 
 
Description: Identify, discover, or locate threats and/or hazards through active and passive 
surveillance and search procedures. This may include the use of systematic examinations and 
assessments, sensor technologies, or physical investigation and intelligence. 
SITUATIONAL ASSESSMENT 
 
Mission Area: Response 
 
Description: Provide all decision makers with decision-relevant information regarding the 
nature and extent of the hazard, any cascading effects, and the status of the response. 
SUPPLY CHAIN INTEGRITY AND SECURITY 
 
Mission Area: Protection 
 
Description: Strengthen the security and resilience of the supply chain. 
THREATS AND HAZARD IDENTIFICATION 
 
Mission Area: Mitigation 
 
Description: Identify the threats and hazards that occur in the geographic area; determine 
the frequency and magnitude; and incorporate this into analysis and planning processes so as 
to clearly understand the needs of a community or entity. 
 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
46 
 
 
 
The Transportation Emergency Support Function (ESF #1) ensures the coordination of 
transportation and transportation route restoration during and after local emergencies and 
disasters. In addition, it ensures transportation support for agencies requiring access and egress 
to significant events. 
Primary Agency 
 
Cheyenne Transit Program 
 
Others as assigned by CEO 
ESF #1 Coordinator 
The ESF #1 coordinator(s) staffs a position in the EOC and reports to the EOC when activation 
occurs.   
 
Director of Transit or designee 
Support Agencies 
 
Laramie County School District 1 
 
Laramie County School District 2 
 
Public Works 
 
Building and Development departments 
 
Fire Services 
 
Law Enforcement / Public Safety 
Key Response Core Capability:  Critical Transportation 
Responsibilities 
1. ESF #1 is designed to provide transportation support to assist in local incident 
management. Activities within the scope of ESF#1 includes: 
a. Coordinate transportation response in emergencies and disasters. 
b. Report damage to transportation infrastructure as a result of the incident. 
c. Coordinate alternate transportation services. 
d. Coordinate the restoration and recovery of the transportation infrastructure. 
e. Coordinate and support prevention, protection, response, recovery, and mitigation 
among transportation infrastructure stakeholders at the local level. 
f. Provide first responder/family transportation. 
 
 
Emergency Support Function #1: TRANSPORTATION 
TRANSPORTATION (Emergency Support Function #1) 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
47 
 
2. Duties of support agencies: 
a. Provide additional transportation methods and personnel  
b. Clear transportation routes to access affected areas 
c. Provide parking staging areas with signage  
d. Assist with re-routing of resources  
e. Assist with damage assessment 
f. Maintain traffic control and flow 
g. Assist with access/egress routes in evacuation (See Annex D – Emergency 
Evacuation) 
h. Process and coordinate requests for civil transportation prior to or during an 
incident 
i. 
Provide support resources to maintain the mission of ESF #1 
j. 
Assist in coordination of transportation of elderly and special needs population 
3. The ESF #1 coordinator is a member of the EOC team and will report to the EOC in any 
emergency requiring activation of the EOC. The ESF #1 coordinator will establish 
communications with all support agencies needed to assist with transportation services. 
4. The coordinator will establish and maintain a list of resources and inventory needed to 
carry out its mission. 
5. Planning Assumptions: 
a. Damage may occur to the transportation infrastructure in the event of an 
emergency or disaster.  The type and degree of damage will determine the 
effectiveness and efficiency of the response and recovery efforts. 
b. Initial response may be difficult to coordinate but will improve with the clearing of 
access routes.  
c. If demand on the transportation system exceeds the capabilities of the county, 
assistance will be required from the state. 
6. Assist the EOC team with planning, pre-positioning and staging of transportation 
resources in the case of evacuation or relocation of citizens and responders. 
RESPONSE 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #1 during the response phase 
of an emergency. 
1. Priorities of ESF #1: 
a. Life safety 
b. Maintain infrastructure 
c. Provide citizen and responder assistance 
d. Seek alternative methods of transportation 
RECOVERY 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #1 during the recovery phase 
of an emergency: 
1. ESF #1 will continue to assess transportation infrastructure and make recommendations 
of restoration to the EOC and local officials. 
2. The coordinator will continue to direct support agencies in transportation needs. 
3. ESF #1 will coordinate the needs and progress of transportation with other emergency 
support functions/agencies involved in recovery. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
48 
 
 
 
 
 
The Communications Emergency Support Function (ESF #2) ensures the coordination and use 
of the local communications systems for the dissemination of emergency traffic and disaster 
information. ESF #2 coordinates the exchange of vital information between local responders, local 
government and all support agencies. 
Primary Agency 
 
Laramie County Combined Communications Center 
 
Others as assigned by CEO 
ESF #2 Coordinator 
The ESF #2 coordinator(s) staffs a position in the EOC and reports to the EOC when activation 
occurs. 
 
Director of Laramie County Combined Communications Center or designee 
Support Agencies 
 
Laramie County Information Technology Department 
 
City of Cheyenne Information Technology Department 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
 
Amateur Radio Emergency Services (ARES) 
 
Radio Amateur Civil Emergency Services (RACES) 
 
National Weather Service (NWS) 
Key Response Core Capabilities:  Operational Communications, Intelligence and Information 
Sharing, Infrastructure Systems 
Responsibilities 
1. ESF #2 is responsible for: 
a. Dispatch and directing emergency services in times of emergencies and disaster. 
b. Coordination of the communications network in emergencies and disasters. 
c. Reporting damage to communications infrastructure as a result of the incident. 
d. Coordinating alternate communication methods. 
e. Coordinate and support prevention, preparedness and mitigation among 
communications infrastructure users at the local level. 
f. Being the main warning point for Laramie County providing warning and activation 
of the Outdoor Warning System should the system need to be manually activated. 
 
 
Emergency Support Function #2: COMMUNICATIONS 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
49 
 
2. Duties of support agencies: 
a. Support telecommunications/data communications 
i. Maintain contact with local data centers that provide the necessary 
infrastructure to continue operations during an event or disaster. 
b. Provide additional communication methods and personnel 
c. Provide alternative public warning systems 
d. Provide technology support to maintain equipment 
e. Maintain equipment inventory 
f. Provide current weather and atmospheric forecasts along with any changes to 
either of them 
g. Assist with damage assessments of communications infrastructure 
h. Assist with restoration and recovery of communications infrastructure 
i. 
Provide support resources to maintain the mission of ESF #2 
3. The ESF #2 coordinator is a member of the EOC team and will report to the EOC in any 
emergency requiring activation of the EOC. The ESF #2 coordinator will establish 
communications with all support agencies needed to assist with providing communications 
during an emergency or disaster. 
4. The coordinator will establish and maintain a list of resources and inventory needed to 
carry out its mission. 
5. Planning Assumptions: 
a. Damage may occur to the communications infrastructure in the event of an 
emergency or disaster.  The type and degree of damage will determine the 
effectiveness and efficiency of the response and recovery efforts. 
b. If demand on the communication systems exceeds the capabilities of the county, 
assistance will be required from the state.  Alternate communications systems 
through the Wyoming Office of Homeland Security must be coordinated through 
proper procedure. 
RESPONSE 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #2 during the response phase 
of an emergency 
1. ESF #2 will provide necessary communication systems to properly disseminate 
information. 
a. Alternate communications systems through the Wyoming Office of Homeland 
Security must be coordinated through proper procedure.  
2. Priorities of ESF #2: 
a. Life safety 
b. Maintain communication infrastructure 
c. Provide responder assistance 
d. Seek alternative methods of communication 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
50 
 
RECOVERY 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #2 during the recovery phase 
of an emergency: 
1. ESF #2 will continue to coordinate local actions to provide for the restoration of the 
telecommunications interface between local agencies, local and state governments, and 
with outside organizations.  This includes the federal government, private nonprofit 
organizations, and business/industry. 
a. Communications restoration includes radio, telecommunications, broadcast 
cables, and electronic networks. 
2. The coordinator will continue to direct support agencies in communication needs. 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan

The Public Works & Engineering Emergency Support Function (ESF #3) defines the role of local 
public works and engineering departments in providing resources related to lifesaving or life 
protection prior to, during and following a disaster. 
Primary Agency 
 
Public Works Department (City and/or County) 
 
Others as assigned by CEO 
ESF #3 Coordinator 
The ESF #-3 coordinator(s) staffs a position in the EOC and reports to the EOC when activation 
occurs. 
 
Director of Public Works or designee 
Support Agencies 
 
Fire Departments 
 
Parks & Recreation 
 
Risk Management (City & County) 
 
Human Resources (City & County) 
 
Traffic 
 
Public Utilities 
 
Cheyenne-Laramie County Public Health 
 
Zoning 
 
Planning 
 
Laramie County Information Technology Department 
 
City of Cheyenne Information Technology Department 
 
ESF #10 – Oil & Hazardous Materials Response 
Key Response Core Capabilities:  Infrastructure Systems, Critical Transportation, 
Environmental Response/Health and Safety, Logistics and Supply Chain Management, Fatality 
Management, Mass Care Services, Mass Search and Rescue Operations 
 
 
Emergency Support Function #3: PUBLIC WORKS AND 
ENGINEERING 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
52 
 
Responsibilities 
1. ESF #3 is structured to provide public works and engineering-related support for the 
changing requirements of local incident management to include preparedness, 
prevention, response, recovery and mitigation actions.  Activities within the scope of this 
function includes: 
a. Conducting pre and post incident assessments of public works and infrastructure. 
b. Provide technical assistance to include engineering expertise, construction 
management and contracting and real estate services. 
c. Provide emergency repair of damaged infrastructure and critical facilities in 
coordination with elected officials and department heads. 
2. Duties of support agencies: 
a. Assist with physical debris removal and labor 
b. Provide additional available equipment 
c. Coordinate additional labor forces 
d. Assist with traffic and pedestrian routing 
e. Assist with engineering and design 
f. Provide documentation and assessment assistance 
3. The ESF #3 coordinator is a member of the EOC team and will report to the EOC in any 
emergency requiring activation of the EOC. The ESF #3 coordinator will establish 
communications with all support agencies needed to assist with public works services. 
4. The ESF #3 coordinator is responsible for maintaining and updating an inventory of 
personnel, vehicles and equipment used in the restoration of infrastructure. 
5. The coordinator will manage and provide technical advice in the clearance, removal and 
disposal of debris from public properties. Coordinate with ESF #10 for the management 
of contaminated debris. 
6. The coordinator will work with the state and federal government to maintain public 
assistance programs involving public works.  
7. Planning Assumptions: 
a. Damage may occur to the public works infrastructure in the event of an emergency 
or disaster.  The type and degree of damage will determine the effectiveness and 
efficiency of the response and recovery efforts. 
b. Where appropriate, activities to restore infrastructure (e.g. debris removal, 
temporary housing mission, etc.) are closely coordinated with ESF #11 – 
Agriculture and Natural Resources. 
c. The coordinator will have to assess damages and prioritize response with limited 
response capabilities. 
d. If calls for assistance exceed the capabilities of public works, mutual aid from local 
and state agencies must be requested. 
RESPONSE 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #3 during the response phase 
of an emergency. 
1. Identify established priorities for emergency clearance of debris to allow passage of 
equipment, personnel and supplies into the affected area immediately following a disaster. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
53 
 
2. Resource requests will be directed through the EOC and the elected official(s) with the 
affected jurisdiction. Private resources may be needed to assist in control and response 
efforts.  
3. Priorities of ESF #3: 
a. Life Safety 
b. Emergency repair of infrastructure 
c. Debris removal 
RECOVERY 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #3 during the recovery phase 
of an emergency. 
1. The ESF #3 coordinator will continue to provide support during the recovery phase of 
disaster operations. 
2. The duties of the coordinator and support agencies will not change during the recovery 
phase of operations. Support agencies will continue to provide assistance as needed to 
the ESF #3 coordinator as requested.   
3. Activities to be performed during recovery operations (See responsibilities of ESF #3). 
4. Identify established priorities for the clearing, repair or reconstruction of damaged 
transportation routes including streets, roads, bridges, ports, waterways, airfields and 
other vital transportation facilities. 
STANDARD OPERATING PROCEDURES 
The following criteria should be addressed during all phases of an emergency:  
1. The Public Works Director of the affected jurisdiction will be the ESF #3 coordinator. In 
cases where multiple jurisdictions are affected, a unified command structure will be 
utilized. The unified command will make joint decisions and provide joint priorities during 
all phases of emergency management.   
2. The ESF #3 coordinator will work with any and all support agencies to provide the needed 
assistance to perform the required operations.  
3. The ESF #3 coordinator will provide assistance to all other ESF’s that may require physical 
or technical assistance to perform their function.  
4. The ESF #3 coordinator will work with Human Resources to ensure 24-hour staffing 
patterns for the restoration of infrastructure operations immediately following a disaster. 
5. The ESF #3 coordinator will assist the EOC to provide procedures for the restoration of 
critical public services and facilities.  
6. The ESF #3 coordinator will provide procedures for the demolition or stabilization of 
damaged structures and facilities designated as immediate hazards to public health and 
safety. 
7. The ESF #3 coordinator will provide for the pre-positioning of resources when it becomes 
apparent that skilled construction workers, engineers, vehicles and construction materials 
will be necessary. 
8. The ESF #3 coordinator will assist in implementation of structural and infrastructure 
mitigation efforts before, during and after a disaster. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
54 
 
 
The Fire Fighting Emergency Support Function (ESF #4) coordinates response to fires, rescue, 
medical assistance, extrication, mass casualty incidents as well as warning and evacuation prior 
to a disaster.  ESF #4 coordinates the fire response to any disaster.  
Primary Agency 
 
Fire Department/Fire District 
 
Others as assigned by CEO 
ESF #4 Coordinator 
The ESF #4 coordinator(s) staffs a position in the EOC and reports to the EOC when activation 
occurs. 
 
Fire Chief(s) of the affected jurisdiction(s) or liaison 
Support Agencies 
 
City of Cheyenne Public Works 
 
Laramie County Public Works 
 
Cheyenne-Laramie County Public Health  
 
Laramie County Fire Warden’s Office 
 
Risk Management (City & County) 
 
ESF #2 (Communications) 
 
ESF #9 (Search & Rescue) 
 
ESF #10 (Oil & Hazardous Materials Response) 
 
ESF #13 (Public Safety & Security) 
Key Response Core Capabilities:  Fire Management and Suppression, Situational Assessment, 
Infrastructure Systems, Operational Communications, Critical Transportation, Mass Search and 
Rescue Operations, Public Health and Medical Services 
Responsibilities 
1. ESF #4 is charged with life safety and protection/minimization of damage to property by 
providing emergency services to the following incident types: 
a. Suppression of wildland, rural and urban fires 
b. Emergency medical assistance 
c. Terrorism/WMD response 
d. Rescue/extrication services 
e. Mass casualty events 
f. Aircraft accidents 
g. Warning and evacuation 
Emergency Support Function #4: FIRE FIGHTING 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
55 
 
2. Duties of support agencies: 
a. Provide technical assistance with structural and engineering needs 
b. Provide heavy equipment and operators to assist in rescue operations 
c. Provide assistance with public medical and mass casualty response 
d. Provide communications support 
e. Coordinate the use of fire services in search and rescue operations 
f. Assist in public safety needs 
g. Provide technical assistance in risks involved in fire response and employee safety 
h. Provide the reference location of an inventory of vehicles and equipment that will 
be available for firefighting activities, including the last date of revision. 
3. The ESF #4 coordinator is a member of the EOC team and will report to the EOC in any 
emergency requiring activation of the EOC. The coordinator will establish communications 
with all support agencies needed to complete the duties of ESF #4. 
4. The ESF #4 coordinator is responsible for maintaining and updating an inventory of 
personnel, vehicles and equipment being used or are available for firefighting activities 
during a disaster. 
5. Each fire department is responsible for providing fire/rescue functions for its jurisdictional 
area and for assisting in providing service outside its jurisdictional area as per oral 
agreement, mutual aid agreements, and/or memorandums of understanding. 
6. The Laramie County Fire Warden or their designee will coordinate any wildland or rural 
fire response requests to and from the Division of Forestry through the EOC and the CEO. 
7. If the emergency expands beyond the jurisdictional boundaries or beyond the capabilities 
of the fire department within its jurisdiction, additional resources and/or requests for 
assistance will be routed through the LCCCC and coordinated through the ESF #4 
coordinator. 
8. Planning Assumptions: 
a. Fire departments having local jurisdiction have the primary responsibility and 
capability or mechanism in place to respond to emergencies within their respective 
jurisdiction. 
b. All fire departments within Cheyenne and Laramie County participate in a mutual 
aid agreement whereby resources can be requested to respond to emergencies 
outside of their jurisdictions. 
c. Fire departments can expect assistance from other city and county agencies upon 
request. 
d. Training levels and levels of expertise vary among departments within the county. 
Special operations response such as Hazmat, Terrorism/WMD and aircraft 
accidents will require mutual aid responses by the jurisdiction most capable of 
mitigating the incident. 
e. ICS is utilized by all fire departments within Cheyenne and Laramie County. 
f. Vulnerabilities: 
i. Insufficient operating budgets 
ii. Need for continued and updated training due to hazard increase 
iii. Inadequate water supply in many areas of the county 
iv. Lack of specialized equipment in some departments 
v. Equipment failures 
vi. Limited manpower for extended operations 
vii. Extended response times to outlying areas 
viii. Weather related problems affecting response and recovery 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
56 
 
RESPONSE 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #4 during the response phase 
of an emergency or disaster: 
1. Priorities of ESF #4: 
a. Life safety 
b. Minimize property damage 
2. On-scene operations will coordinate with the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency 
Operations Center through standard communications networks or networks provided by 
ESF #2 (Communications). 
 
 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
57 
 
 
Information and Planning Emergency Support Function (ESF #5) is coordinated by Emergency 
Management and supports overall activities of the local governments within Cheyenne and 
Laramie County for local jurisdiction incident management. ESF #5 provides the core 
management and administrative functions in support of Emergency Management activities, 
Emergency Operations Center, Joint Information Center, and other agencies and departments. 
Primary Agency 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
ESF #5 Coordinator 
The ESF #5 Coordinator(s) staffs a position in the EOC and monitors potential or developing 
incidents and supports the efforts of regional and field operations. 
 
Emergency Management Staff 
Support Agencies 
 
All agencies involved in local preparedness, response, recovery, mitigation and security 
efforts. 
Key Response Core Capabilities:  Situational Assessment, Public Information & Warning, 
Operational Coordination, Intelligence & Information Sharing, Risk & Disaster Resilience 
Assessment, Threats & Hazard Identification, Operational Communications 
Responsibilities 
1. ESF #5 is responsible for coordinating resources to meet the core capabilities outlined in 
the National Response Framework and in turn support the 5 Mission Areas.  ESF #5 will 
provide additional coordination of: 
a. Public Warning and Emergency Information 
b. Emergency Communications 
c. Damage Assessments 
d. Individual Family Assistance 
e. Continuity of Government 
 
 
Emergency Support Function #5: INFORMATION & PLANNING 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
58 
 
2. The ESF #5 Coordinator is a member of the Chief Elected Official(s) staff and will: 
a. Develop Standard Operating Procedures for opening and staffing the EOC in times 
of disaster. 
b. Provide emergency communications within the EOC. 
c. Coordinate information within the EOC and help facilitate local officials in decision 
making. 
d. Act as a liaison to the State and Federal partners. 
Organizational Structure 
Command Support:  ESF #5 supports the command function by assisting command and general 
staff with incident action planning activities, information gathering, administrative tasks, logistics 
coordination, and financial support functions.   
Operations: ESF #5 provides assistance to the Operations Section Chief and Operations Branch 
Director positions to: 
 
Coordinate the Human Services, Infrastructure Support, Emergency Services, and 
Mitigation and Community Recovery Branches (various ESF’s also provide key staff for 
these areas) 
 
Process requests for assistance  
 
Initiate and manage the mission assignment and/or the reimbursement agreement.  
Planning:  ESF #5 assists the Planning Section Chief and Planning Branch Director by assisting 
with the collection, evaluation, dissemination, and use of information as it pertains to the 
prevention, response actions, and status of resources for the incident.  The Planning Section is 
responsible for the local incident action planning process for each operational period.  Preparation 
includes: 
 
Documenting incident priorities 
 
Establishing the operational period and tempo 
 
Developing contingency, long-term, demobilization, and other plans related to the incident 
as needed. 
 
Coordinates with the Wyoming Office of Homeland Security and other agencies with 
special technical capabilities to request support for geospatial intelligence, modeling and 
forecasting. 
Logistics:  ESF #5 assists the staff of the Logistics Section Chief to manage the control and 
accountability of local supplies and equipment.   The Logistics Section coordinates closely with 
ESF #7 (Logistics) and implements the Logistics Management Support Annex.  The management 
of resources includes:  
 
Resource ordering 
 
Delivery of equipment, supplies, and services 
 
Resource tracking 
 
Assistance with facility location and operations 
 
Transportation coordination 
 
Operation of information technology systems and other administrative services.   
The Logistics Section Chief also coordinates with the Wyoming Office of Homeland Security, as 
necessary, to identify and deploy State assets.   

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
59 
 
Finance/Administration:  ESF #5 assists staff for the Finance and Administration Section Chief 
to monitor funding requirements and incident costs. The Finance/Administration Section is 
responsible for employee services, including security for personnel, facilities and assets.   
Mission Areas 
1. Prevention:  The capabilities necessary to avoid, prevent, or stop a threatened or actual 
act of terrorism. 
2. Protection:  The capabilities necessary to secure the homeland against acts of terrorism 
and manmade or natural disasters. 
3. Mitigation:  The capabilities necessary to reduce loss of life and property by lessening 
the impact of disasters. 
4. Response:  The capabilities necessary to save lives, protect property and the 
environment, and meet basic human needs after an incident has occurred. 
5. Recovery:  The capabilities necessary to assist communities affected by an incident to 
recover effectively. 
 
 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
60 
 
 
 
 
The Mass Care, Emergency Assistance, Temporary Housing, and Human Services Emergency 
Support Function (ESF #6) supports local and NGO efforts to address non-medical mass care, 
provide emergency assistance, temporary housing, and human services needs of individuals 
and/or families impacted by incidents of local significance. 
Primary Agency 
 
American Red Cross 
 
Other as assigned by CEO 
ESF #6 Coordinator 
The ESF #6 coordinator(s) staffs a position in the EOC and reports to the EOC when activation 
occurs. 
 
American Red Cross Director or designee 
Support Agencies 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
 
Laramie County School District #1 
 
Laramie County School District #2  
 
ESF #2 (Communications) 
 
ESF #3 (Public Works and Engineering) 
 
ESF #5 (Information and Planning) 
 
ESF #7 (Logistics Management and Resource Support) 
 
ESF #8 (Public Health and Emergency Medical Services) 
 
ESF #11 (Agriculture and Natural Resources) 
 
Cheyenne Kiwanis Club 
 
Salvation Army 
 
Cheyenne-Laramie County Public Health 
 
NGO’s providing assistance to disaster victims 
Concept of Operations 
Initial response activities focus on meeting urgent mass care needs of victims.  Recovery efforts 
can be initiated concurrently with response activities. Close coordination is required among those 
local agencies and NGOs participating in response and recovery operations. 
Emergency Support Function #6: MASS CARE, EMERGENCY 
ASSISTANCE, TEMPORARY HOUSING, AND HUMAN SERVICES 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
61 
 
Key Response Core Capabilities:  Mass Care Services; Logistics & Supply Chain Management; 
Public Health & Medical Services; Critical Transportation; Fatality Management Services; 
Housing 
Responsibilities 
All agencies under ESF #6 work together to provide life-sustaining assistance to victims.  The four 
primary functions for ESF #6 and what each area covers are: 
1. Mass Care: 
a. Sheltering 
i. Emergency sheltering includes the use of pre-identified shelter sites in 
existing structures, creation of temporary facilities or use of similar facilities 
should evacuation be necessary. 
b. Feeding 
i. Feeding is provided to victims through a combination of fixed sites, mobile 
feeding units and bulk distribution of food. 
ii. In Laramie County, most food provisions will be provided by NGOs and 
coordinated through ESF #6. 
c. Other supporting activities to support emergency needs of victims 
i. Setup and coordinate bulk distribution sites with local government entities 
and NGOs 
1. Distribution of emergency relief items needed to meet urgent needs 
are distributed through these sites and established within the 
affected area. 
ii. Reunification of families 
1. Disaster Welfare Information (DWI) collects and provides 
information regarding individuals within the affected area to 
immediate family members which also aids the reunification 
process. 
2. Emergency Assistance: 
a. Coordination of voluntary organizations and unaffiliated volunteers 
b. Organizing and coordinating donations both solicited and unsolicited 
c. Coordinate emergency first aid consisting of basic first aid and referral to 
appropriate medical personnel at designated sites 
d. Providing support to individuals with disabilities and others with access and 
functional needs 
e. Coordinating non-congregate and transitional sheltering 
f. Provide support to children in disasters 
g. Coordinate support for mass evacuations 
h. Provide support for the rescue, transportation, care, shelter, and essential needs 
of household pets and service animals 
i. 
Coordination of federal assistance in support of non-medical mass care services 
and gathering information related to sheltering and feeding in the impacted area 
3. Temporary Housing: 
a. Addresses both short and long term housing assistance 
b. Identification and provision of safe, secure, functional and physically-accessible 
housing 
c. Identify a plan of action to provide housing assistance 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
62 
 
4. Human Services 
a. Coordinate disaster assistance programs that help survivors address unmet 
disaster caused needs and/or non-housing losses through loans and grants 
b. Coordinate and provide: 
i. Supplemental nutrition assistance 
ii. Crisis counseling 
iii. Disaster case management 
iv. Disaster unemployment 
v. Disaster legal services 
vi. Other state and Federal human services programs and benefits to 
survivors 
5. Duties of support agencies: 
a. Provide additional support and coordination to meet the responsibilities of ESF #6. 
b. ESF #8 and ESF #11 will assist with the coordination of mass care services to 
provide for the safety and well-being of individuals with access and functional 
needs support during evacuations and sheltering. 
c. Household Pets and Service Animals 
i. The local response community recognizes the varying and special 
requirements of individuals that require and utilize service animals and is 
committed to ensuring that the physical and mental health needs of these 
individuals are appropriately addressed and that the individuals and service 
animals remain together during to the maximum extent possible during the 
evacuation, transport, sheltering, or the delivery of other services and in 
accordance with the requirements of the ADA. 
RESPONSE 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #6 during the response phase 
of an emergency. 
1. Priorities of ESF #6: 
a. Life Safety 
b. Food and Sheltering 
c. Safety of citizens 
RECOVERY 
The following are criteria set forth for Emergency Support Function #6 during the recovery phase 
of an emergency: 
1. ESF #6 will continue to assess and coordinate required resources through the transition 
from the Response to Recovery phase. 
2. The coordinator will continue to direct support agencies in transportation needs.

available for mutual aid response in and/or near Laramie County.

supplies, telecommunications, personnel and needed supplies to assist disaster 
victims during the recovery phase.

to and mitigating the effects of a disaster.

response and recovery.

disaster response and recovery logistical support.

available at the local level.

infrastructure sectors to mitigate cascading failures and risks.

The External Affairs Emergency Support Function (ESF #15) provides accurate, coordinated, 
timely, and accessible information to affected audiences, including governments, media, the 
private sector, and all of the local populace.  The local populace would also include children; those 
with disabilities and other access and functional needs; and individuals with limited English 
proficiency.  ESF #15 will also coordinate local actions to provide the required external affairs 
support to local incident management elements.  Working with the Joint Information Center (JIC), 
ESF #15 establishes support positions to coordinate communications to the audiences that 
provide critical emergency information, crisis communications, and public affairs support. 
Primary Agency 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency

occurs.

 
American Red Cross 


 
ESF #2 (Communications) 
 
ESF #5 (Information and Planning) 
 
ESF #8 (Public Health and Medical Services) 
 
Joint Information Center 
Key Response Core Capabilities:  Public Information and Warning; Operational Coordination; 
Intelligence and Information Sharing; Risk Management for Protection Programs and Activities; 
Situational Assessment 
Responsibilities 
1. During an incident, all authorities share responsibility for communicating information 
regarding the incident to the public.  The ESF #15 coordinator must ensure that the 
following objectives are met: 
a. Delivery of incident preparedness, health, response, and recovery instructions to 
those directly affected by the incident. 
Emergency Support Function #15: EXTERNAL AFFAIRS 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
79 
 
b. Dissemination of incident information to the public, including children; those with 
disabilities and other access and functional needs; and individuals with limited 
English proficiency populations. 
2. The ESF #15 provides a supporting mechanism to develop, coordinate, and deliver 
information to the public related to: 
a. Assistance to the incident-affected area 
b. Departmental/agency response 
c. Other preparations 
d. Protective measures 
e. Impact on non-affected areas 
3. ESF #15 support shall monitor, collect, analyze, and transmit information from various 
public information sources during an event or disaster. 
STANDARD OPERATING PROCEDURES 
The SOP establishes procedures and protocols for ESF #15 External Affairs to support incident 
management during an incident requiring a coordinated response among several different 
involved parties including State and Federal government.  ESF #15 integrates Public Affairs, the 
Joint Information Center (JIC), governmental agencies, and private sector communications under 
the coordinating auspices of External Affairs.  An activation of ESF 15 during an incident requiring 
a coordinated response will facilitate accurate, coordinated, and timely information to affected 
audiences, including governments, media, the private sector, and the local populace. 
Objectives 
There are two (2) objectives for the SOP in ESF #15: 
1. It will be used to formulate external affairs incident action plans and procedures that will 
help save lives and protect the health and safety of the public, responders, and recovery 
workers. 
2. It will be used as a framework to guide messaging to protect property; mitigate damages 
and impacts to individuals, communities, and the environment; and facilitate recovery 
information for individuals, families, businesses, governments, and the media. 
Concept of Operations 
Through coordination with the affected partners and other entities, ESF #15 will establish and 
conduct external affairs activities to support response and recovery operations.  These activities 
and supporting organizations will be scalable and flexible with respect to the incident situation.  
The ESF #15 team will develop and execute an external affairs strategy to support the Incident 
Action Plan (IAP).  A central feature of this concept, consistent with the incident command system, 
is a unified planning component that brings unity of effort to all strategic communications planning 
and product development.  All participating communicators must ensure messaging reflects both 
unknown issues and facts as they become acknowledged.  In an emergency, many issues will fall 
within the unknown category and communicators should be careful not to over-reassure and 
should note uncertainty remains.  Transparency and consistent messaging across agencies is 
critical to public confidence and community cohesion. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
80 
 
Daily Operations 
After establishment of ESF #15 and the roles of each participant is completed, the ESF #15 
coordinator will develop a daily external affairs routing which is critical to the smooth flow of 
information and will assist with synchronization.  Participants must be flexible and work to ensure 
communications objectives and initiatives are accommodated and timed for optimum delivery.  A 
daily communications summary will be prepared by the ESF #15 team with input from all involved 
partners (government, private, and NGOs).  These messages shall be well crafted and designed 
to convey the broad, overarching issues facing residents in the affected area(s).  These messages 
should address both current and anticipated issues.  The ESF #15 team shall take into 
consideration all forms of media outlets when crafting messages. 
 
 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
81 
 
 
 
Introduction 
The Incident Annex identifies and outlines specific incidents that have the highest probability of 
occurring within Laramie County.   Each annex provides a description of each event, the effects 
it could potentially have within the county and an outline of roles, responsibilities, and coordinating 
efforts for each type of event.   
These annexes are designed to be an outline of activities implemented during each type of event 
in coordination with the Planning Annexes used within the Cheyenne/Laramie County jurisdiction.   
Each Emergency Support Function and the agencies coordinating within them may also have 
internal plans they coordinate with in cooperation with the Cheyenne/Laramie County Plans.   
 
 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County EOP – Incident Specific Annexes 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
82 
 
 
Purpose 
In accordance with the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan, this annex serves 
the as the framework for responding to an earthquake emergency. This annex establishes a 
comprehensive format outlining proactive measures to be executed before, during, and 
immediately following a seismic event. The outlined activities within this plan are designed to 
facilitate a coordinated and effective response, ensuring the safety and well-being of the 
community while minimizing the impact of the earthquake. This document aims to provide 
emergency personnel with a strategic guide to navigate the complexities of an earthquake 
scenario, emphasizing the importance of preparedness, response coordination, and swift action 
in the face of such a crisis. 
Nature of the Event 
In coordination with the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan, it is imperative 
to comprehend the nature and potential ramifications of an earthquake event. Described as a 
sudden, violent shaking or movement of the Earth's surface, this phenomenon arises from the 
abrupt displacement of rock masses, typically occurring within the upper 10 to 20 miles of the 
Earth's surface. 
The seismic hazards associated with earthquakes are multifaceted and include the following 
elements: 
1. Ground Motion: This encompasses the vibration and shaking of the ground during an 
earthquake, constituting the most far-reaching effect that inflicts significant damage to 
buildings, structures, and infrastructure. 
2. Ground Surface Fault Rupture: The ground shaking results from the rupture of a fault 
beneath the surface. This rupture can lead to the surface of the ground opening up, with 
potential openings of up to 20 feet, intensifying the impact of the seismic event. 
3. Liquefaction: This phenomenon entails the temporary loss of ground strength, causing 
the ground to behave as a viscous fluid akin to quicksand, rather than a solid structure. 
4. Landslides: Earthquakes can trigger landslides, involving rock falls and the sliding of rock 
fragments on steep slopes, thereby adding to the overall geological risks. 
5. Secondary Hazards: Consequences of earthquakes may extend beyond the seismic 
event itself and encompass secondary hazards such as fire outbreaks, hazardous material 
releases, or structural failures. Recognizing and addressing these potential secondary 
hazards is integral to a comprehensive emergency management response. 
Situation and Assumptions 
Situation 
Considering the unique seismic situation in Laramie County, where earth tremors are infrequent 
but not entirely unheard of, it is essential to acknowledge the broader context of seismic activity 
in the region. While the western part of Wyoming experiences several tremors annually, Laramie 
County itself has encountered sporadic instances of small earthquakes or tremors over the past 
century, with no reported damage. 
Annex A - Earthquake 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
83 
 
Despite the relatively low frequency of seismic events, it is crucial for Emergency Responders to 
recognize that Laramie County is not exempt from the potential impact of earthquakes. The United 
States Geological Survey highlights the active ground movement area associated with the 
Yellowstone Caldera, introducing an additional layer of consideration for emergency 
preparedness. 
Although earthquake risk is not classified as high, the potential consequences of a moderate to 
extreme quake could pose a significant threat to the area's infrastructure. Therefore, measures 
must be in place to address the specific challenges posed by earthquakes, ensuring a proactive 
and effective response to minimize potential devastation and safeguard the community and its 
infrastructure. 
Assumptions 
1. Seismic activities follow certain patterns based on historical records.  
2. Earthquakes will come with no warning. 
3. Certain types of buildings and infrastructure may be more vulnerable to earthquake 
damage.  
4. High population density can lead to increased casualties and challenges in evacuation. 
5. Earthquakes may disrupt communication infrastructure.  
6. Earthquakes may trigger secondary hazards such as landslides, tsunamis (in coastal 
areas), or aftershocks.  
7. Effective coordination among various agencies and organizations is crucial.  
8. Earthquake recovery can be a prolonged and complex process.  
Concept of Operations 
In the aftermath of an earthquake event and dependent upon the extent of Cheyenne/Laramie 
County Emergency Operations Center (CLC-EOC) activation, Emergency Support Function 
(ESF) coordinators and Chief Executive Officers (CEOs) of the affected jurisdictions are to report 
to the EOC for the purpose of organizing the preliminary damage assessment priorities. The 
deployment of Rapid Assessment Teams is imperative to kickstart comprehensive evaluations, 
as referenced in Support Annex 4. 
Should circumstances necessitate, the CEO is empowered to issue a formal 'Declaration of 
Emergency.' Subsequently, Emergency Management is tasked with initiating contact with the 
Wyoming Office of Homeland Security (WOHS) to begin local/state coordination.  

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
84 
 
EOC Activation 
ESF Duties under Level 1 EOC Activation 
In the event of Level 1 activation at the CLC-EOC, the CLC-EOC Manager will initiate the 
activation of all 15 Emergency Support Functions (ESFs). As the situation unfolds, the staffing 
levels at the CLC-EOC may be adjusted, either increased or decreased, based on the scale, 
complexity, or duration of the incident or event. Refer to the Emergency Operations Plan (EOP) 
Base Plan, specifically the section on EOC Activation, for a breakdown of all activation levels 
and the associated procedural steps. 
ESF Duties under Level 2 or 3 EOC Activation 
For an activation Level of 2 or 3 of the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Center 
(CLC-EOC) due to a Inclement Weather incident, the following ESFs are expected to be activated: 
 
ESF #01 (Transportation): ensures the coordination of transportation and transportation 
route restoration during and after local emergencies and disasters. In addition, it ensures 
transportation support for agencies requiring access and egress to significant events. 
 
ESF #02 (Communications): Assesses disruption to communications systems due to 
power failure or other infrastructure damage. Ensures the coordination and use of the local 
communications systems for the dissemination of emergency traffic and disaster 
information. 
 
ESF #05 (Information & Planning): Provides the core management and administrative 
functions in support of Emergency Management activities, Emergency Operations Center, 
Joint Information Center, and other agencies and departments. 
 
ESF #06 (Mass Care Services): Supports local and NGO efforts to address non-medical 
mass care, provides emergency assistance, temporary housing, and human services 
needs of individuals and/or families impacted by incidents of local significance. 
 
ESF #07 (Resource Support): Provide logistical and resource support to response 
agencies which is critical in their efforts to respond and recover from natural or man-made 
disasters. 
 
ESF #08 (Public Health and Medical Services): Ensures provision of comprehensive 
medical care to disaster victims and victims of mass health related events including 
veterinary and/or animal health issues. 
 
ESF #09 (Search and Rescue): Coordinates the deployment of certified Search and 
Rescue groups to provide specialized life-saving assistance during emergency events 
within their jurisdictions. 
 
ESF #13 (Public Safety and Security): Integrates local public safety and security 
capabilities and resources to support the full range of law enforcement incident 
management and response activities in times of disaster.  
 
ESF #15 (External Affairs): Provides accurate, coordinated, timely, and accessible 
information to affected audiences, including governments, media, the private sector, and 
all of the local populace. 
Primary ESF:  
There is no established primary ESF for an inclement weather incident. The CLC-EOC may 
choose to identify a primary ESF based on the individual incident. The primary ESF may transition 
during response as conditions change.

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
88 
 
 
Purpose 
The objective of this annex is to enhance the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations 
Plan by describing policies and procedures tailored to response and recovery efforts during 
Inclement Weather emergencies. This annex has been created to foster a whole community 
response approach from local agencies, aiming to minimize the risk of inclement weather 
consequences and expedite the restoration of essential services in the aftermath of significant 
inclement weather events.  
Given the diverse climate in Laramie County, inclement weather remains a constant threat 
throughout the year, dictated by the seasons. This annex serves as a crucial tool in Laramie 
County’s preparedness, allowing coordination through the unique challenges posed by inclement 
weather and fortify our community's resilience against its impacts.  
When discussing emergency preparedness, it is imperative to acknowledge that winter weather 
constitutes a distinct category within the spectrum of Inclement Weather Emergencies. Annex H 
has been carefully written to align with the established and adopted Laramie County/City of 
Cheyenne snow plan. It is important to note that this annex operates in tandem with, rather than 
supersedes, the Snow Plan Annex, Annex H. This strategic alignment ensures a comprehensive 
and cohesive approach to emergency response, particularly in the face of winter-related 
challenges. 
Situation and Assumptions 
Situation 
Laramie County confronts recurrent severe weather incidents annually, encompassing a 
spectrum of inclement weather emergencies such as, but not limited to: Tornados, Floods, 
Thunderstorms, Heatwaves, and Extreme Cold. This annex is crafted with a particular focus on 
employing planning, response, mitigation, and, in extreme scenarios, evacuation/sheltering 
measures to enhance the safety of Laramie County residents. The potential for damage is 
multifaceted, encompassing flooding and erosion, ice accumulation, disruption of public services 
and communications, and the peril of damage or destruction to public and private property, with 
the gravest consequence being the loss of life. Urban areas, in particular, face heightened 
vulnerability to the repercussions of heavy rain and flash flooding. 
1. Tornados: A tornado, characterized by a violently rotating column of air extending from a 
thunderstorm to the ground, poses a severe threat, causing potentially devastating 
damage in its path. 
2. Floods: Floods arise when water surpasses normal levels, submerging land and property. 
This can result from heavy rainfall, rapid snowmelt, storm surges, or the failure of dams 
or levees. 
3. Thunderstorms: Thunderstorms, marked by atmospheric disturbances featuring 
lightning, thunder, heavy rain, and occasionally hail or tornadoes, develop when warm, 
moist air rises and interacts with cooler air. 
4. Heatwaves: Prolonged periods of excessively high temperatures, often accompanied by 
high humidity, define heatwaves. These events present serious health risks, leading to 
heat-related illnesses and imposing stress on ecosystems. 
Annex B – Inclement Weather  

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
89 
 
5. Extreme Cold: Extreme cold refers to temperatures significantly below the regional 
average, resulting in freezing conditions. Exposure to extreme cold can lead to frostbite, 
hypothermia, and other cold-related health issues. This annex underscores the imperative 
of comprehensive measures to address the diverse challenges posed by each of these 
inclement weather phenomena, aiming to safeguard the well-being and resilience of the 
community in Laramie County 
Assumptions 
1. There is a need for extensive coordination among various local agencies to ensure a 
unified and effective response to inclement weather emergencies. 
2. Laramie County experiences inclement weather throughout the entire year, with different 
seasons bringing varying weather challenges.  
3. Emergency management efforts are geared towards protecting lives and minimizing harm 
to the community. In extreme flooding conditions, Laramie County may require state and 
federal     assistance. 
4. Specific considerations and challenges associated with densely populated areas that 
need to be addressed in emergency planning. 
5. Urban areas in Laramie County are particularly vulnerable to the impacts of heavy rain 
and flash flooding. 
6. Emergency management plans should be flexible and adaptable to the changing weather 
patterns. A significant number of flooding casualties may occur due to the effects of severe 
weather. 
7. There is an awareness of the potential health risks associated with specific weather 
events, such as heatwaves leading to heat-related illnesses and extreme cold posing risks 
of frostbite and hypothermia. 
8. There is a commitment to protecting both public infrastructure and individual assets during 
emergency situations. 
Concept of Operations 
The Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan emphasizes a local-first approach 
to the initial response to all incidents. When the scale of an incident surpasses local capabilities, 
the established declaration process enables the solicitation of additional aid from state agencies 
or, if needed, the federal government at the request of the Governor. See SECTION “X” FOR 
THE DECLARATION PROCCESS. 
Given Laramie County's diverse climate and the persistent threat of inclement weather throughout 
the year, this annex underscores the importance of flexibility in emergency plans. By addressing 
a spectrum of weather-related challenges, from tornadoes to floods and extreme cold, the 
community aims to fortify its resilience.  
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
90 
 
Watches and Warnings 
 
Flash Flood Watch: A Flash Flood Watch is issued by the National Weather Service 
(NWS) when conditions are favorable for the development of flash floods. The NWS 
specifies the area(s) and times for which the watch is in effect. This alert indicates that 
there is a potential for rapid and life-threatening flooding in the designated areas, and 
people should stay informed and be prepared to take action if necessary. 
 
Flash Flood Warning: A Flash Flood Warning is issued when flooding is actually 
occurring or is imminent. The warning may include various flood hazards such as 
thunderstorm effects, road damage, or high waters that make roads impassable. This alert 
is a signal for people in the affected areas to take immediate action to protect themselves 
and their property from the imminent danger of flash flooding. 
 
Severe Thunderstorm Watch: A Severe Thunderstorm Watch is issued when conditions 
are favorable for the development of severe thunderstorms. The NWS specifies the 
area(s) and times for which the watch is in effect. This alert indicates that severe 
thunderstorms, with associated hazards like lightning, high winds, hail, heavy rain, or 
tornadoes, could occur in the designated areas, and people should stay informed and be 
prepared for rapidly changing weather conditions. 
 
Severe Thunderstorm Warning: A Severe Thunderstorm Warning is issued when severe 
thunderstorms are actually occurring or are imminent. The warning includes information 
about specific severe weather hazards, such as lightning, high winds, hail, heavy rain, or 
downpours leading to flash flooding and tornadoes. This alert prompts people in the 
affected areas to take immediate safety precautions. 
 
Tornado Watch: A Tornado Watch defines an area where tornadoes and other severe 
weather are possible in the next several hours. It does not indicate that tornadoes are 
imminent but serves as a warning for people to stay alert and prepared for the potential 
occurrence of tornadoes. During a tornado watch, individuals are advised to turn on local 
TV or radio, activate weather radios, ensure access to safe shelter, and inform friends and 
family about the potential tornado threat. 
 
Tornado Warning: A Tornado Warning is issued when a tornado has been spotted or 
when Doppler radar indicates a thunderstorm circulation that could spawn a tornado. 
When a Tornado Warning is issued for a specific town or county, immediate safety 
precautions are necessary. Local NWS offices issue tornado warnings, and residents in 
the warned area should seek safe shelter immediately. 
 
Wind Chill Advisory: A Wind Chill Advisory is issued when the combination of cold 
temperatures and wind speeds could result in conditions that may be harmful to exposed 
skin. 
 
Wind Chill Watch: A Wind Chill Watch is issued when there is the potential for extremely 
cold wind chill values in the upcoming hours or days. 
 
Wind Chill Warning: A Wind Chill Warning is issued when wind chill conditions are 
expected to be hazardous and may pose a significant threat to life and property. 
 
Heat Advisory: A Heat Advisory is issued when high temperatures and high humidity 
levels combine to create conditions that may lead to heat-related illnesses. While not as 
severe as an Excessive Heat Warning, a Heat Advisory indicates that individuals should 
exercise caution and take steps to prevent heat-related health issues. 
 
Excessive Heat Warning: An Excessive Heat Warning is issued when extremely high 
temperatures and high humidity levels are expected to create hazardous conditions that 
may pose a significant risk to health and safety. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
91 
 
Dam Failure/Causes 
Dam failures can result from various causes, including overtopping, foundation defects, piping 
and seepage failures, and conduit and valve issues. Overtopping may occur due to poor spillway 
design, settling of the dam crest, or spillway blockage, leading to high water levels during heavy 
rainfall. Foundation defects result from settling in the dam foundation, slope instability, uplift 
pressures, and seepage, causing structural instability. Piping and seepage failures involve 
internal erosion from seepage, erosion along hydraulic structures, animal burrows, and dam 
structure cracks. Conduit and valve failures stem from problems with dam valves and conduits. 
Other dam failures may arise from miscellaneous causes, such as earthquakes, landslides, 
extreme storms, heavy snow-melt, equipment malfunction, structural damage, or sabotage. Dam 
incidents are categorized into Type 1 (slowly developing), Type 2 (rapidly developing), and Type 
3 (instantaneous failure). A Type 3 failure requires immediate notification and evacuation due to 
the imminent threat, with a short time before water starts to rise. 
In the specific context of Crystal or Granite Reservoirs, a dam breach could result in water 
reaching Cheyenne within 3-5 hours, necessitating urgent emergency response and evacuation 
procedures. 
EOC Activation 
ESF Duties under Level 1 EOC Activation 
In the event of Level 1 activation at the CLC-EOC, the CLC-EOC Manager will initiate the 
activation of all 15 Emergency Support Functions (ESFs). As the situation unfolds, the staffing 
levels at the CLC-EOC may be adjusted, either increased or decreased, based on the scale, 
complexity, or duration of the incident or event. Refer to the Emergency Operations Plan (EOP) 
Base Plan, specifically the section on EOC Activation, for a breakdown of all activation levels 
and the associated procedural steps. 
ESF Duties under Level 2 or 3 EOC Activation 
For an activation Level of 2 or 3 of the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Center 
(CLC-EOC) due to an Inclement Weather incident, the following ESFs are expected to be 
activated 
 
ESF #01 (Transportation): ensures the coordination of transportation and transportation 
route restoration during and after local emergencies and disasters. In addition, it ensures 
transportation support for agencies requiring access and egress to significant events. 
 
ESF #02 (Communications): Assesses disruption to communications systems due to 
power failure or other infrastructure damage. Ensures the coordination and use of the local 
communications systems for the dissemination of emergency traffic and disaster 
information. 
 
ESF #03 (Public Works): Defines the role of local public works and engineering 
departments in providing resources related to lifesaving or life protection prior to, during 
and following a disaster. 
 
ESF #4 (Firefighting): Coordinates response to fires, rescue, medical assistance, 
extrication, mass casualty incidents as well as warning and evacuation prior to a disaster.  
ESF #4 coordinates the fire response to any disaster.
