 
Critical infrastructure disruptions   
 
Road closures and alternative routes  
 
Protective actions   
 
Signs and symptoms of contamination   
 
Ongoing incident operations   
 
Confirm jurisdictional communication strategies to prevent any misinformation sharing. 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
119 
 
 
Purpose 
The purpose of this plan is to define and implement procedures for events concerning terrorism 
and possible use of Weapons of Mass Destruction (WMD) against public safety personnel and 
citizens of the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County. These weapons may be classified as, but 
not limited to, chemical, biological, radiological nuclear and explosive (CBRNE). This Annex will 
also be a reference to public safety first responders outlining responsibilities and procedures in 
confirming presence of CBRNE weapons, and notifying the appropriate federal, state, or local 
agency. 
Terrorism is defined as: “The unlawful use of force or violence against persons or property to 
intimidate or coerce a government, the civilian population, or any segment thereof in furtherance 
of political, religious, or social objectives.” 
This plan will further outline the transition from “crisis management” to “consequence 
management” roles of involved agencies. 
Concept of Operations 
General 
This plan is based on the premise that the functions of agencies involved in an event will have 
operations that parallel their day-to-day activities. The same resources may be employed 
elsewhere depending on the need and function. Activities that do not contribute directly to the 
event may be suspended or redirected to the event operations for the duration. 
Assumptions 
No single agency within Cheyenne or Laramie County possesses the level of expertise nor has 
the assets to act unilaterally on a widespread response to a suspected act of terrorism, particularly 
if it is determined to be a WMD incident. 
The response to terrorism incidents is divided into two separate and distinct phases: 
 
Crisis Management: Measures to identify, acquire, and plan for the use of resources 
needed to anticipate, prevent, and/or resolve a threat or act of terrorism 
 
Consequence Management: Measures to protect public safety and health, restore 
essential government services, and provide emergency relief to governments, 
businesses, and individuals affected by the consequences of terrorism. 
o Emergency Management is specifically concerned with its responsibilities for 
consequence management, the effects of terrorism incidents, training of first 
responders, and the safety of all participants throughout the incident. 
o The Incident Command System (ICS) and NIMS will be utilized during these 
events.  
o If appropriate Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) is not available, entry into the 
suspected contaminated area may be delayed until the material dissipates to a 
level allowing safe entry by responders. 
 
 
Annex F - Terrorism Incident Response 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
120 
 
o An act of terrorism involving weapons of mass destruction may produce major 
consequences that would overwhelm the capabilities and resources of local public 
safety. First responders dispatched to these incidents may not know that they are 
entering a terrorist or WMD event, therefore, incapacitating them. 
Scope 
 
This plan will apply to all threats or acts of terrorism within Laramie County including Albin, 
Burns, Cheyenne, and Pine Bluffs.  
 
This plan will apply to all public safety entities within Cheyenne and Laramie County 
including assistance provided by private sector contractors engaged in assisting public 
safety within this plan. 
 
This plan will be considered as a special event Annex for Terrorism to the 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency’s Emergency Response 
Plan. 
Policies 
The lead federal agency responsible for crisis management for an incident involving WMD will be 
the Department of Justice, which has delegated this task to the Federal Bureau of Investigation.  
The law enforcement agency of the jurisdiction will be the lead agency locally for incidents 
involving threats or suspected use of WMD. Support agencies will be the Cheyenne Police 
Department, Laramie County Sheriff’s Office, and Wyoming Division of Criminal Investigation, 
Cheyenne Fire Rescue Department, Laramie County Fire Warden, and Laramie County Fire 
Districts. 
The affected governmental jurisdiction along with EMA has primary responsibility for 
consequence management of incidents involving WMD, and will be supported by the ESF’s, 
NGO’s and private organizations.  Other state and local agencies may be tasked if it is determined 
their support is required. 
Upon determining that a WMD incident has occurred, the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency 
Response Plan may be activated. The City Council or Board of County Commissioners may 
declare that an emergency exists whenever the threat or actual occurrence of an incident is, or 
threatens to be, of sufficient severity and magnitude to require significant fiscal assets or 
resources to be committed to the incident, and a coordinated response is required.  The Federal 
Bureau of Investigation Resident Agent in Charge (FBIRAC) will be notified immediately when a 
threat of any significance or actual incident has been determined to have occurred. All local crisis 
management will be coordinated with the FBIRAC. 
1. During the Crisis Management phase: 
a. The Incident Command System (ICS) will be used to provide law enforcement 
direction and control during the Crisis Management operations. The appropriate 
law enforcement commander will designate the Incident Commander. 
b. The Incident Commander will immediately notify the Director of the 
Cheyenne/Laramie 
County 
Emergency 
Management 
Agency, 
so 
that 
Consequence Management actions can be initiated. The CLCEMA Director or a 
representative will notify the Director of the Wyoming Office of Homeland Security 
(WOHS) and advise that office of the incident. Situation reports will be completed 
and forwarded in a timely manner. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
121 
 
c. The Federal Bureau of Investigation (FBI), in cooperation with the law enforcement 
agency in command, will exercise authority for managing the federal response at 
the incident site. 
d. If requests from the FBI are for state law enforcement assistance and non-law 
enforcement assistance, a local or state liaison officer will be appointed and the 
request forwarded to the Wyoming Office of Homeland Security. 
i. All emergency management assets will be directed to assist the FBI as 
much as is practical until the Crisis Management phase has concluded. 
e. If a suspected terrorist incident has occurred where injuries and/or death have or 
may have occurred, Consequence Management activities may be implemented 
prior to or in conjunction with Crisis Management activities. In this situation, 
activities will be conducted under the control and supervision of the Incident 
Commander. 
f. Once the Crisis Management activities are completed to the satisfaction of the FBI 
and appropriate state and local law enforcement agencies, Consequence 
Management activities will begin, and will be coordinated through the Laramie 
County Emergency Management Agency. 
2. During the Consequence Management phase: 
a. The FBI will no longer be responsible and direction and control activities will revert 
to the local and state Emergency Management systems. 
b. Procedures for requesting State and federal assistance for response and recovery 
from a terrorist incident will follow the current procedures as outlined in the 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Response Plan. 
i. Once local and mutual aid resources are depleted, the Mayor or Chairman 
of the Board of Laramie County Commissioners will request assistance in 
coordination with the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management 
Agency and the investigating jurisdiction through the WOHS Duty Officer. 
Assurances will be made that all city and county resources have been 
utilized. 
ii. The WOHS will appoint a liaison to monitor the situation pending the 
declaration. 
Based on the severity of the incident, the Director of WOHS would be contacted and requested 
to recommend to the Governor that an emergency be declared. The Governor, based on relevant 
information, may issue a State Declaration of Disaster, and request that the President of the 
United States issue an emergency and/or Major Disaster Declaration for Wyoming. These actions 
are necessary to receive federal disaster aid as provided for in the Stafford Act (Public Law 93-
288 as amended). 
Direction and Control 
General 
Direction and control of the emergency response and recovery operation within Cheyenne and 
Laramie County will be exercised in accordance with the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency 
Response Plan and Annexes. 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
122 
 
The Chief Elected Officials will, as soon as practical, request assistance from the State of 
Wyoming based on the escalation of the situation, and in coordination with the Cheyenne/Laramie 
County Emergency Management Agency and the investigating jurisdiction.  The Federal Bureau 
of Investigation will be notified immediately of any threats or acts of terrorism in accordance with 
this annex. 
Upon response of the FBI, the Special Agent in Charge (SAC), and local law enforcement 
authorities will establish one centralized unified command post and exercise direction and control 
appropriate and consistent with their respective responsibilities to effectively accomplish all law 
enforcement duties. 
It will be the responsibility of local government to take the lead role with support provided from 
the State of Wyoming and any federal government assets.  Primary and support agencies will 
assure that their respective personnel are trained and prepared to operate in the event regular 
personnel are unavailable. They will identify alternate and back up personnel, understanding the 
standard operating procedure for response to suspected or known terrorist events, and ensure 
that appropriate guidelines contain sufficient detail so that these personnel can use them in 
performance of their duties. 
EOC Activation 
ESF Duties under Level 1 EOC Activation 
In the event of Level 1 activation at the CLC-EOC, the CLC-EOC Manager will initiate the 
activation of all 15 Emergency Support Functions (ESFs). As the situation unfolds, the staffing 
levels at the CLC-EOC may be adjusted, either increased or decreased, based on the scale, 
complexity, or duration of the incident or event. Refer to the Emergency Operations Plan (EOP) 
Base Plan, specifically the section on EOC Activation, for a breakdown of all activation levels and 
the associated procedural steps. 
ESF Duties under Level 3 or 2 EOC Activation 
For an activation Level of 3 or 2 of the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Center 
(CLC-EOC) due to a Terrorism Incident, the following ESFs are expected to be activated: 
 
ESF #05 (Information & Planning): Gathers information about the incident and identifies 
the resources required to respond  
 
ESF #07 (Resource Support): Locates and provides supplies to emergency response 
personnel, as needed 
 
ESF #10 (Hazardous Materials): Handles the control and containment of a threatened or 
actual intentional release of hazardous materials  
 
ESF #13 (Public Safety and Security): Enforces protective actions and provides safety 
and security around the incident site  
 
ESF #15 (External Affairs): Provides accurate updates and messaging to the public 
Primary ESF:  
ESF #13 should consider the following for terrorism incident response:   
 
Serves as the primary ESF for a terrorism incident in coordination with the Federal Bureau 
of Investigations (FBI)   
 
Mobilizes the resources and capabilities of public safety agencies and security to protect 
life, property, and maintain law and order  

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
123 
 
 
Supports the FBI’s investigation and intelligence activities 
Relevant ESF’s: 
ESF #05 should consider the following for terrorism incident response:   
 
Working with ESF #13 (Public Safety and Security), establish guidelines for approval of 
release of information.   
 
Develop and distribute reports on the status of operations and impacts, including Situation 
Reports as needed.   
 
Ensure the timely warning of Laramie County residents and visitors of potential or actual 
impacts, including relaying protective actions ordered by law enforcement  
 
Organize information shared through other ESFs to establish situational awareness of 
impacts across the community, including:   
 
Status of critical infrastructure  
 
Sheltering and mass care needs  
 
Any reports of civil unrest  
 
Develop a process for information sharing between local incident management agencies 
and other appropriate sectors that can offer support.  
ESF #07 should consider the following for terrorism incident response:   
 
Assist with terrorism-specific resource requests such as Regional Emergency Response 
Teams (RERT) or National Guard Civil Support Teams (CSTs).  
ESF #10 should consider the following for terrorism incident response:   
 
Detect and assess the extent of environmental contamination.   
 
Notify receiving hospitals of the need for lockdown or decontamination protocols.   
 
Manage clean-up of hazardous materials that are released.  
ESF #15 should consider the following for terrorism incident response:   
 
Ensure involvement from law enforcement agencies to understand sensitive data 
breaches and ensure public information does not compromise an ongoing investigation.   
 
Organize and coordinate unified messaging for the public about the incident. 
 
Confirm jurisdictional communication strategies to prevent any misinformation sharing. 
Organization and Assignment of Responsibilities 
1. First Responder 
a. As a first responder to weapons of mass destruction or terrorist incidents, it is 
important to conduct an initial size up of the area, and be aware of the potential for 
secondary devices, which are intended to injure or kill rescue workers within the 
area. This is usually a conventional weapon, but may be others. 
b. Attempt to obtain as much information as possible concerning the incident 
including: 
i. How many people are involved? 
ii. If more than one person is involved, are there similar symptoms presented? 
iii. Where is the reporting party? 
iv. Is the reporting party in or near contact with the victim(s)? 
c. Approach to a suspected scene must always be done from an uphill and up wind 
direction. 
d. Avoid all liquid and vapor clouds. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
124 
 
e. Use Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) if available. If not available, do not enter 
a scene. 
f. Personal protective equipment will be of sufficient level as to properly protect the 
responder at the level indicated by the threat. 
2. Emergency Support Functions (ESFs) 
a. While all agencies may be involved in some aspect of the response to a terrorist 
incident, there may be certain Emergency Support Functions that are anticipated 
to play a more active role in the event. 
b. In regards to the nature of the emergent situation, the law enforcement agency 
with jurisdiction will act as the primary functional agency for Crisis Management, 
with the coordination and assistance of the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency 
Management Agency. The Wyoming Office of Homeland Security may be 
requested to provide a coordination role, but specifically, will assist in coordination 
with the Governor’s Office. 
Direction and Control 
General 
Direction and control of the emergency response and recovery operation within Cheyenne and 
Laramie County will be exercised in accordance with the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency 
Response Plan and Annexes. 
The Chief Elected Officials will, as soon as practical, request assistance from the State of 
Wyoming based on the escalation of the situation, and in coordination with the Cheyenne/Laramie 
County Emergency Management Agency and the investigating jurisdiction.  The Federal Bureau 
of Investigation will be notified immediately of any threats or acts of terrorism in accordance with 
this annex. 
Upon response of the FBI, the Special Agent in Charge (SAC), and local law enforcement 
authorities will establish one centralized unified command post and exercise direction and control 
appropriate and consistent with their respective responsibilities to effectively accomplish all law 
enforcement duties. 
It will be the responsibility of local government to take the lead role with support provided from 
the State of Wyoming and any federal government assets.  Primary and support agencies will 
assure that their respective personnel are trained and prepared to operate in the event regular 
personnel are unavailable. They will identify alternate and back up personnel, understanding the 
standard operating procedure for response to suspected or known terrorist events, and ensure 
that appropriate guidelines contain sufficient detail so that these personnel can use them in 
performance of their duties. 
 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
125 
 
 
In the coordination of wildfire response efforts, key agencies in Laramie County play vital roles. 
These include Laramie County Fire Districts 1-10, the Wyoming State Forestry Division, F.E. 
Warren Air Force Base Fire, Wyoming Air National Guard Fire Department, Cheyenne Fire 
Rescue, Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency, and the Laramie County 
Combined Communications Center. This collaborative effort involves the coordination of various 
resources and expertise to effectively address wildfire incidents. Additionally, cooperating 
agencies, such as WYDOT, Wyoming Highway Patrol, Wyoming Highway Patrol Dispatch, 
Cheyenne Police Department, Laramie County Sheriff’s Office, Holly Frontier Refinery Fire 
Brigade, Laramie County Road & Bridge Department, Wyoming Office of Homeland Security, 
Wyoming Department of Environmental Quality, and the National Weather Service, contribute to 
the overall response strategy, ensuring a comprehensive and unified approach to wildfire 
management and mitigation. 
Purpose 
This annex serves as a comprehensive guide delineating the roles, responsibilities, and 
coordinating mechanisms for managing wildland fire incidents within Laramie County, Wyoming. 
Encompassing the incorporated areas of the City of Cheyenne, and the towns of Albin, Burns, 
and Pine Bluffs, this overview addresses the potential significant impacts wildland fires can have, 
particularly in the Wildland Urban Interface (WUI). Local, state, and federal agencies prepare for 
imminent wildland fires, with the Wyoming State Forestry Division overseeing fire management 
on 3.6 million surface acres of state trust land and collaborating on cooperative fire management 
on private and federal lands statewide. Laramie County, protected by nine fire districts and three 
full-time paid departments, requires coordinated county-wide suppression efforts. Rapid and 
efficient response efforts are essential to extinguish wildland fires, provide temporary shelter, and 
assess community and infrastructure impacts. Refer to ESF 4 – Firefighting, within this plan for 
additional details. 
Objectives 
Provide pertinent information to local, regional, state, and federal decision-makers regarding the 
state's planned response to wildland fires. 
Define coordination strategies, illustrating how local, regional, state, tribal, and federal entities 
collaborate throughout wildland fire mitigation, preparedness, response, and recovery. 
Direct users to applicable functional annexes within the State Emergency Management Plan and 
other relevant documents. 
Situation 
Laramie County faces the potential for large wildland urban interface and wildland fires, often 
triggered by human activities. The expanding wildland-urban interface heightens the risk of 
substantial losses. 
 
 
Annex G – Wildland Fire 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
126 
 
Assumptions 
Response to significant fires may be complex due to hazardous materials or extreme weather 
conditions. Planning and preparedness for wildland-urban interface and wildland fire, across all 
emergency management phases, are collective responsibilities of all fire entities protecting 
Laramie County and the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency. Response 
capabilities may be compromised by factors such as inadequate equipment, a shortage of trained 
personnel, insufficient funding, environmental conditions, multiple concurrent incidents, 
equipment malfunctions, human error, jurisdictional or legal issues, and incident location and 
access challenges. Notification may be received from private citizens, first responders en-route 
or on scene, federal, state, or local agencies, or other sources. All local city and county emergency 
services are dispatched through the Combined Communications Center in Cheyenne 
("DISPATCH"), following established protocols and agreements. 
EOC Activation 
ESF Duties under Level 1 EOC Activation 
In the event of Level 1 activation at the CLC-EOC, the CLC-EOC Manager will initiate the 
activation of all 15 Emergency Support Functions (ESFs). As the situation unfolds, the staffing 
levels at the CLC-EOC may be adjusted, either increased or decreased, based on the scale, 
complexity, or duration of the incident or event. Refer to the Emergency Operations Plan (EOP) 
Base Plan, specifically the section on EOC Activation, for a breakdown of all activation levels and 
the associated procedural steps. 
ESF Duties under Level 3 or 2 EOC Activation 
For an activation Level of 3 or 2 of the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Center 
(CLC-EOC) due to a wildfire incident, the following ESFs are expected to be activated: 
 
ESF #01 (Transportation): Initiates temporary road closures and alternative routes as 
needed; supports planning for evacuations  
 
ESF #05 (Information and Planning): Gathers information about the incident and 
identifies resources required to respond   
 
ESF #06 (Mass Care): Coordinates temporary sheltering and assistance   
 
ESF #07 (Resource Support): Locates and provides supplies to emergency response 
personnel as needed 
 
ESF #08 (Public Health and Medical Services): Addresses emerging medical needs 
due to wildfire impacts, including smoke/poor air quality  
 
ESF #11 (Agriculture, Natural, and Cultural Resources): Assesses potential and actual 
impacts to natural and cultural resources   
 
ESF #15 (External Affairs): Provides accurate updates and messaging to the public 
Primary ESF:  
ESF #04 should consider the following for wildfire incident response:   
 
Serves as the primary ESF for a wildfire incident  
 
Coordinates wildland firefighting operations and requests for mutual aid 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
127 
 
Relevant ESF’s: 
ESF #01 should consider the following for wildfire incident response:   
 
Provide information on the safest and most reliable routes for emergency personnel 
 
Assess the transportation impacts from the wildfire and the need for additional road 
closures, alternate routes, or other protective actions.  
 
Monitor conditions that may restrict people from obtaining critical services or supplies, 
especially vulnerable populations who may be isolated due to road closures.   
 
Work with ESF #03 (Public Works) to provide equipment and personnel for clearing 
roadways of debris as needed.  
 
Provide information on road closures and alternate routes for distribution to the public in 
coordination with ESF #15 (External Affairs) through the Joint Information System (JIS).  
ESF #05 should consider the following for wildfire incident response:  
 
Ensure adequate alert and warning of the public  
 
Work with Incident Command to communicate protective actions to the public to protect 
and mitigate impacts to people and property at risk  
 
When the CLC-EOC is activated in advance of the incident, host a virtual planning meeting 
to determine the state of preparedness and potential impacts from the incident, including 
impacts to special events, government services, and the whole community.  
ESF #06 should consider the following for wildfire incident response:   
 
Determine the need to establish “clean air” centers to serve people significantly impacted 
by smoke and poor air quality, or other vulnerable groups.   
 
Organize temporary shelter accommodations for vulnerable populations and/or evacuees.   
 
Consider the need for a Family Assistance Center (FAC) if an incident and subsequent 
evacuations/displacement increase.   
 
Monitor protective actions that may impact vulnerable populations who need additional 
support to evacuate or implement the recommended actions.  
ESF #07 should consider the following for wildfire incident response:   
 
Identify and acquire resources to support “clean air” shelters and temporary 
accommodations for evacuees as needed.  
ESF #08 should consider the following for wildfire incident response:   
 
Monitor protective actions that could affect healthcare providers and vulnerable groups 
that receive treatments (such as dialysis) or rely on power for medical equipment.   
 
Identify potential impacts to vulnerable populations and communicate additional resource 
needs to the CLC-EOC to support equitable response. Vulnerable populations for a 
wildfire (and smoke impacts) may include: 
 
Seniors/elderly people   
 
People with significant medical needs   
 
Pregnant women   
 
People with pre-existing pulmonary or cardio vascular conditions  
 
Support the development of public messaging on health impacts from smoke and poor air 
quality for distribution to the public in coordination with ESF #15 (External Affairs) through 
the JIS.  
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
128 
 
ESF #11 should consider the following for wildfire incident response:   
 
Assess potential impacts to natural resources during initial incident response and advise 
on immediate protective measures.   
 
Provide appropriate response actions to conserve, rehabilitate, recover, and restore 
natural, cultural, and historic resources impacted by the incident.  
 
Provide just-in-time wildfire mitigation actions to landowners that they can implement 
safely to reduce the impacts to their property from wildfire.   
 
Monitor air quality levels and support the development of public messaging on air quality 
monitoring for distribution to the public in coordination with ESF #15 (External Affairs) 
through the JIS.  
ESF #15 should consider the following for wildfire incident response:   
 
Provide emergency information to the public focused on life safety. Topics may include:  
 
Air quality information and subsequent health impacts  
 
Preparing for evacuation or other protective actions  
 
Home hardening and preparation  
 
Clean air centers, temporary shelters, and FAC locations, if established  
 
Extent of damage and casualties  
 
Safety information (driving, road closures, etc.)  
 
Post-wildfire actions and potential secondary hazards 
 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
129 
 
Purpose 
Under the authority of Laramie County, the City of Cheyenne, and the Wyoming State 
Government, the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency (CLCEMA) has 
been given the directive by all governments to recommend: 
 
Full closure of business due to weather conditions 
 
Daytime closure due to inclement weather conditions 
 
Delayed opening or early release due to unsafe road and/or travel conditions 
Objective 
 
This plan will be implemented when travel and weather conditions are degraded to a point 
that the health, safety and welfare of the employees of the City of Cheyenne, Laramie 
County and State of Wyoming become adversely affected. 
 
To establish a notification procedure for the implementation of government closures or 
delayed opening due to travel conditions.  
Scope 
This plan affects all of Laramie County Government, Laramie County School District 1, the City 
of Cheyenne, and Wyoming State government offices in and near Cheyenne.  
This plan does not affect the Towns of Albin, Burns, Pine Bluffs, Laramie County School District 
2 or F.E. Warren AFB. However, recommendations can be made to any entity within Laramie 
County for the purposes of this plan.  
Plan 
This plan supersedes all other plans by date. 
CLCEMA will continually monitor weather conditions affecting Southeast Wyoming and all cities 
and towns within. Emergency Management will contact any agency it feels will enhance the 
decision to close government operations. Including but not limited to: 
 
National Weather Service 
 
Wyoming Highway Patrol 
 
Wyoming Office of Homeland Security/Office of the Governor of Wyoming 
 
Laramie County School District 1 
 
Laramie County School District 2 
 
Laramie County Fire Districts 
 
Cheyenne Fire and Rescue 
 
Laramie County Public Works 
 
Cheyenne Police Department 
 
American Medical Response 
 
Black Hills Energy 
 
High West Energy 
 
Cheyenne Regional Medical Center EMA 
 
Veteran Affairs Emergency Manager 
Annex H - Snow Plan 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
130 
 
 
WYDOT 
 
Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Department 
 
Board of Public Utilities 
 
Cheyenne Laramie County Combined Communications Center 
During periods of severe weather, regardless of time of day, EMA will contact any of those 
agencies listed to determine a course of action to be recommended and implemented by all 
governments.  Additional agencies may be included in the Snow Plan conference calls in the 
event there are significant issues, i.e. Blizzard Warnings, Major power outages, sheltering needs, 
etc.  Information about weather affecting the general area will be gathered and recommendations 
from all agencies will be compiled to give the best possible directive. The directive will be based 
upon the concern for the health, safety and welfare of the citizens.  
EMA will work in conjunction with and agree with the majority of the following entities: 
 
Wyoming Office of Homeland Security 
 
Office of the Governor of Wyoming 
 
Laramie County School District 1 
 
City of Cheyenne 
 
Laramie County 
Closure Decision 
As information has been compiled, EMA will contact and recommend a course of action to the 
following: 
1. Chairman – Laramie County Board of County Commissioners 
a. Laramie County Public Works 
2. Mayor – City of Cheyenne 
a. City of Cheyenne Public Works 
3. State of Wyoming 
a. Wyoming Office of Homeland Security 
4. Laramie County School District 1 
Media Notification 
When a closure or advisory is decided, EMA will send out notification and details of the decision 
using the Emergency Notification System.  This will also send the notification messages to the 
Public Information Officer group that includes representatives from the various City and County 
agencies. 
Emergency Snow Plan Zone (1) – All Day Closure/Delay/Early Release 
Emergency Snow Plan Zone (2) – Closure/Delay/Early Release 
Emergency Snow Plan Zone (3) – Closure/Delay/Early Release 
Each jurisdiction is responsible for implementation of their individual snow plan. Agencies should 
pre-designate essential vs. non-essential personnel and determine what level of continuity their 
departments/agencies will operate under.  

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
131 
 
Level of Closure Description  
Laramie County is divided into three (3) areas closing in on the City of Cheyenne (see map). 
Closure/Delay/Early Release are determined by road and weather conditions affecting the area. 
Area 3 is furthest away from Cheyenne and is divided into 2 parts, East and West, separated by 
I-25.  Next is Area 2 followed by Area 1 which is within the city limits of Cheyenne.  Area 1 is 
consistent with the boundary closures of LCSD # 1.  
As severe weather approaches and conditions worsen, a Level 3 Advisory may be placed into 
effect. People living in Area 3 (East, West or both) will be advised to go home and prepare to 
shelter the storm.  Any persons working in Area 3 are advised to move to their homes or shelter 
closer to interior areas. As conditions deteriorate an Advisory for Level 2 and Closure for Level 1 
would follow.  
Significant Weather Event Response Guidelines 
On rare occasions, the county has a significant amount of snow and blizzard conditions, which 
cause major concerns for plowing capabilities, and the response efforts of First Responders for 
911 calls.  In the event this occurs, the following steps will take place to help alleviate double 
efforts and coordinate response assets throughout the City and County.   
1. A virtual or in person Unified Command for Public Safety Response will be established by 
CLCEMA to assist with response and coordination needs. This decision will based on a 
review of each storm situation and with recommendations from agencies listed below.  
CLCEMA will establish the most effective virtual platform for Unified Command and/or 
assist with determining location of Command Post.  Agencies to be included may be: 
a. Dispatch – Both LCCCC & HPD 
b. Police, Sheriff, & Highway Patrol 
c. All Fire Agencies Effected within the County 
d. EMS – AMR, Pine Bluffs, & Burns 
e. Public Works – City, County, & State 
f. EMA Volunteer Teams – If needed 
g. Local Utilities 
2. If needed, ICS position specific personnel will be identified to coordinate with EOC 
representatives for additional requests and planning needs.   
3. The Snow Team will act as the virtual Emergency Operations Center for large-scale 
decision-making and additional asset requests such as State/Military and NGO support.  
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
132 
 
Plan Maintenance 
It is the responsibility of EMA to annually review and update this plan. Changes in other plans 
from different agencies should be forwarded to the EMA office for dissemination.

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
133 
 
 
Introduction 
The Support Annex Assistance Guide outlines specific tasks or functions that may be carried out 
during and after an emergency or a disaster. The guide describes, in specific detail, the 
operational system identified in the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Response Plan that 
will be used to carry out the functions listed. It should be based on the Incident Command structure 
described in the Base Plan.  
These annexes are designed to be the major support system for the EOC. These annexes play 
an important part in the needs of the affected jurisdiction to provide logistics, finance, 
documentation, and provide an overview of the incident.  
 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County ERP – Support Annexes 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
134 
 
 
 
Purpose 
This annex provides basic financial management guidance to jurisdictions within Laramie County 
that respond to a major disaster or emergencies. Its intent is to ensure funds are provided 
expeditiously and financial operations are conducted in accordance with established law; County, 
state and federal policies; regulations; and standards. 
Scope 
The scope of this annex is applicable to all agencies and political subdivisions that may apply 
and/or receive state disaster assistance or emergency/disaster relief funding with, or through, 
Laramie County. 
Concept of Operations 
Financial operations will be carried out under extreme emergency/disaster schedules and political 
pressures, necessitating timely, non-routine procedures with sound financial management 
practices and accountability. 
The City/County Clerk serves as the senior fiscal official during the emergency response phase 
and recovery. The Fiscal Director will direct the disposition and accounting of funds.  They will be 
responsible for keeping the CEO and the Emergency Council informed on the status of all funds 
and current financial issues. That individual will have sufficient delegated authority to respond 
quickly to financial problems and coordinate financial issues.  
All local (county or city) agencies should keep strict accounting of their expenditures during a 
disaster. They must maintain records, receipts, and documents to support claims, purchases, 
reimbursements and disbursements and provide such information immediately to the Fiscal 
Director upon request.  
Timely financial support of the extensive response activities will be crucial to success in saving 
lives and property. Innovative and expeditious means may be employed to achieve the financial 
operational support objectives, however, it is mandatory that generally accepted financial policies, 
principles, and regulations be employed in order to ensure against fraud, waste, abuse, and to 
achieve the proper control and correct use of public funds.  
The standard financial management policies and regulations, which apply to each local and 
county agency and department, should also be used in responding to disasters. The Clerk will 
coordinate financial aspects of an emergency or disaster; however, each local agency or 
department is responsible for providing its own financial management and support to its response 
operations in the field. Funds to cover eligible costs for response activities will be provided through 
reimbursement.  
Each local agency and department is responsible for establishing affective administrative controls 
of funds and segregation of duties for proper internal controls.  A responsible official of each 
should be designated to ensure that actions taken and costs incurred are consistent with 
missions.  
Special care must be taken throughout the emergency response period to maintain logs, formal 
records, and file copies of all expenditures in order to provide clear and reasonable accountability 
Support Annex 1 - Financial Management 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
135 
 
and justification for reimbursement. This will be necessary to facilitate the final closeout and 
support audits of financial records after the emergency phase.  
In the event that an incident is related to a release of hazardous materials, the effected jurisdiction 
may take necessary action against the responsible party to collect reimbursement of costs on 
behalf of local and county agencies. Reimbursement of local government costs will be predicated 
upon reasonable fees for services rendered. 
All jurisdictions should, therefore, be aware that EMA will continue to assist them in obtaining the 
necessary resources to mitigate emergency and disaster events when situations exceed their 
local capabilities. The role of Emergency Management will continue to be one of coordination and 
will not replace the authority or responsibility of local officials to exercise control over emergency 
or disaster events. Funding mechanisms exist at both the local and the County level to deal with 
financial requirements of mitigating these events. Typically, financial responsibility for operations 
in a jurisdiction is the obligation of the impacted local government, unless other arrangements are 
detailed ahead of time. Each emergency and disaster event is different, and thus possible 
reimbursement of related expenses will have to be evaluated on a case-by case basis.  
Responsibilities 
1. Fiscal Director: 
a. Ensure that all financial records are maintained throughout the event or disaster.  
b. Ensure that all on-duty time is recorded for each person staffing the Operational 
Area of the EOC.  
c. Ensure that all on-duty time sheets are collected from Field Level Supervisors or 
Incident Commanders and their staff, who are assigned in the county 
unincorporated and contract areas.  
d. Ensure that there is a continuum of the payroll process for all government 
employees responding to the event or disaster.  
e. Determine purchase order limits for the procurement function in Logistics.  
f. Ensure that workers compensation claims, resulting from the response to the event 
or disaster by county employees, are processed within a reasonable time, given 
the nature of the situation.  
g. Ensure that all travel and expense claims are processed within a reasonable time, 
given the nature of the situation.  
h. Provide administrative support to the EOC Sections as required, in coordination 
with the Personnel Unit.  
i. 
Activate units within the Finance/Administration Section as required; monitor 
section activities continuously and modify the organization as needed.  
j. 
Ensure that all recovery documentation is accurately maintained during the 
response and submitted on the appropriate forms to Federal Emergency 
Management Agency (FEMA) and/or the Governor’s Office and Wyoming Office 
of Homeland Security.  
k. Supervise the Finance/Administration Section. 
2. During the Activation Phase, the Fiscal Director will: 
a. Follow the Generic Checklist located at the EOC.  
b. Ensure that the Finance/Administration Section is set up properly and that 
appropriate personnel, equipment, and supplies are in place.  
c. Based on the situation, activate units within the Section as needed.  
d. Ensure that sufficient staff is available for a 24-hour schedule, or as required.  

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
136 
 
e. Ensure there is coordination with all activated ESF’s within the Operational Area 
for the purpose of gathering and consolidating response cost estimates and other 
related information.  
f. Meet with the Logistics Section Chief and review financial and administrative 
support requirements and procedures; determine the level of purchasing authority 
to be delegated to Logistics Section.  
g. Meet with all Unit Leaders and ensure that Responsibilities are clearly understood.  
h. In conjunction with Unit Leaders, determine the initial action planning objectives 
for the first operational period.  
i. 
Notify the EOC Director when the Finance/Administration Section is operational.  
j. 
Adopt a proactive attitude, thinking ahead and anticipating situations and problems 
before they occur. 
3. During the Operational Phase, the Fiscal Director will: 
a. Ensure that Finance/Administration position logs and other necessary files are 
maintained.  
b. Ensure that displays associated with the Finance/Administrative Section are 
current and that information is posted in a legible and concise manner.  
c. Participate in all Action Planning meetings.  
d. Brief all Unit Leaders and ensure they are aware of the EOC objectives as defined 
in the Action Plan.  
e. Keep the EOC Director, General Staff, and the Emergency Council aware of the 
current fiscal situation and other related matters, on an on-going basis.  
f. Ensure that the Recovery Unit maintains all financial records throughout the event 
or disaster.  
g. Ensure that the Time Keeping Unit tracks and records all agency staff time.  
h. In coordination with the Logistics Section, ensure that the Payables Unit processes 
purchase orders and develops contracts in a timely manner.  
i. 
Ensure that the Compensation & Claims Unit processes all workers’ compensation 
claims resulting from the disaster, in a reasonable time frame, given the nature of 
the situation.  
j. 
Ensure that the Time Keeping Unit processes all time sheets and travel/expense 
claims promptly through budget and payroll office.  
k. Ensure that the Finance/Administration Section provides administrative support to 
other EOC Sections as required.  
l. 
Ensure that all recovery documentation is accurately maintained by the Recovery 
Unit during the response, and submitted on the appropriate forms to the Federal 
Emergency Management Agency (FEMA) and/or the Governor’s Office of 
Emergency Services.  
m. Ensure that the briefing for Section staff upon arrival includes information on the 
demands and what they might confront during their shift.  
 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
137 
 
 
Introduction 
The Logistics Management Support Annex describes the framework within which the overall 
logistics management function operates for Incidents of Significance and describes how Laramie 
County disaster resources fit into this framework. It also describes how Life Lines and Emergency 
Support Functions (ESF’s) interact to provide support to the affected population as well as support 
to the incident management effort. 
Purpose 
The Logistics Management Support Annex: 
1. Describes the ERP logistics management and delivery organizations; 
2. Provides an overview of operations for executing logistics management in  
support of an Incident of Significance; and 
3. Outlines Laramie County logistics management responsibilities in support of county and 
local logistics providers during major events. 
Scope 
The Logistics Management Support Annex identifies the components of the Laramie County 
logistics delivery structure and provides a concept of operations for logistics management in 
support of the ERP.  It also outlines how the various logistics agencies interact with Emergency 
Management during an Incident of Significance. 
Definition 
Logistics management is the process of planning, preparing, implementing, and evaluating all 
logistics functions that support an operation or activity.  Logistics management function is an 
element of ESF #5 (Information and Planning) and ESF #7 (Logistics).  If the Incident requires 
logistics, any or all of the ESFs may be activated. 
Effective logistics management contributes to mission success while ensuring all functions are 
executed in a unified manner to reduce costs, ensure appropriate support actions, and increase 
response capability. Individual logistics functions and associated sub-functions include: 
 
Material Management: Material management includes: requirements processing 
(requisitioning and sourcing), acquisition, asset visibility (resource tracking), receipt, 
storage, handling, material security, accountability, inventory, deployment, issue, 
distribution, recovery, reuse and disposition. 
 
Property Management (Personal Property): Accountability, inventory, disposal, and 
record processing. 
 
Facility Management: Facility selection and acquisition, building services, information 
systems, telecommunications and information technology, safety, public health and 
medical services, and physical security. 
 
Transportation Management: Transportation prioritizing, ordering, sourcing, and 
acquisition; time-phasing plans; fleet management; and movement coordination and 
tracking. 
Support Annex 2 - Logistics  

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
138 
 
Policies 
Material Management 
Logistics personnel find appropriate, time-sensitive, and cost-effective ways to fill the materiel 
requirements developed by operations personnel. Equipment and supplies are provided from 
current Mutual-Aid Agreements, Memorandums of Understanding, or, if necessary, from 
commercial sources. 
The affected City/County provide a central procurement authority for City/County Government. All 
procurement actions are made in accordance with internal financial rules and regulations. 
When activated, ESF #7 serves as the point of contact for requesting materials in support of the 
ERP. ESF #7 is responsible for coordinating with internal and external sources to obtain the 
resources required in the most cost-efficient and expeditious fashion. 
ESF #7 operates under the direction of County Commissioners or CEO of the effected jurisdiction, 
City/County Clerk, and City/County Treasurer.  
Personal Property Management 
All agencies and departments acting within the scope of Disaster Relief and/or Emergency 
Assistance will account for personal property in accordance with their agency property 
management policies.  
If other non-county property is used for incident management operations and this property is lost, 
damaged, stolen, or consumed, the agency who owned the equipment will be reimbursed for the 
cost of such property if a federal disaster is declared and the required documentation is provided 
to the jurisdiction. 
The City/County Clerk directs the disposition of all property owned, including items held by 
organizations that intend to request reimbursement for said property. 
Facility Management 
All facilities and related support necessary for operations is sourced through the following ESFs: 
1. ESF #7 (when activated and requested to do so) supports the requirements for obtaining 
local facilities, facility setup, space management, building services, and general facility 
operations. 
2. ESF #3 (when activated and requested to do so) provides operational support at the 
mobilization centers, staging areas, and distribution sites for all infrastructure and 
engineering service commodities (supplies and equipment) required to support missions. 
3. ESF #2 (when activated and requested to do so) supports emergency telecommunications 
and information technology services for local elements during an Incident of Significance. 
4. ESF #8 (when activated and requested to do so) supports public health and medical 
services for local elements during an Incident of Significance. 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
139 
 
Transportation Management 
ESF #1 serves as the point of contact for requesting transportation assistance in support of 
agencies under the ERP.  Other ESF representatives coordinate transportation requirements with 
ESF #1 representatives. ESF #1 determines the best mode and source of transportation.  Refer 
to ESF #1 for more details. 
Concept of Operations 
Logistical Response Operations 
The structure for logistics depends on the partners that provide resources to support incident-
related operations. Logistics support is provided for protection, preparedness, response, 
recovery, and mitigation actions during all phases of incident management. 
1. Initial Actions 
a. ESF’s are activated and response personnel go to the EOC. 
b. Logistics personnel assigned to EOC perform the following functions: 
c. Participate, with operations and other contingency staffs, in coordination and 
decision-making meetings; 
d. Translate operations-generated requirements into contingency specific logistics 
plans for leadership approval; 
e. Establish communications and coordination among logistics partners to locate any 
necessary centers;  
f. May provide asset tracking; and 
g. Analyze requests for resources to determine cost effective and timely means to 
meet requirements. 
2. Ongoing Actions 
a. Logistics personnel transition into logistics management activities.  They execute 
logistics functions as follows: 
b. Materials Management provides supplies and equipment to the incident(s).  This 
logistical function works with ESFs #6 and #8 to provide non-specialized mass 
care and medical supplies, as well as with other ESFs to provide commercially 
available supplies and equipment. The goal is to provide supplies and equipment 
at the location and in the quantities required in the timeliest and cost-effective 
manner. 
c. Property Management accounts for personal property at all operational levels. 
Laramie County or the affected jurisdiction retains title to accountable property 
unless it is transferred to other jurisdictions. Several agencies have the capability 
to establish receiving and distribution operations at the mobilization centers and 
support property management teams by maintaining property accountability on 
items stored at or issued from these locations. 
d. Emergency communications and information technology support is provided to 
support the National Communication System via ESF # 2. 
3. Demobilization 
a. As response operations begin to diminish, EOC will demobilize the ESFs from their 
respective operations. 
b. The Logistics Sections may remain active for several days following demobilization 
for the following closeout activities: 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
140 
 
c. Coordinating and conducting activities associated with retrieving (or disposing 
locally if no longer serviceable), refurbishing and restocking all city/county assets 
used during the disaster operation; 
d. Ensuring that all equipment and supplies are accounted for in accordance with 
appropriate regulations; and  
e. Participating in internal and external after-action reviews. 
Resources Fulfillment Cycle 
This section of the Annex addresses the overall methodology used to anticipate and subsequently 
fulfill requirements of victims during Incidents of Significance.  
1. Overview 
a. The Logistics Section will follow ICS and NIMS to decide where to locate supplies, 
equipment or personnel closer or further from the Incident.  
2. Process 
a. The ordering, sourcing, transportation, issuing, and movement of City/County 
resources will generally follow the procedural steps outlined below: 
b. Resources needed to provide relief to victims of an Incident of Significance are 
identified. This can be a type of supply, equipment, or a service. 
c. The local jurisdiction tries to fill the need from existing resources. If they do not 
have the resource, they pass the requirement on to the EOC. 
d. If the EOC cannot fulfill the resource needs, the State receives the requirement, 
the State attempts to fulfill the need. 
e. The Operations Section determines if the resource is available in staging areas to 
fulfill the requirement. If the resource is not available, the requirement will be 
passed through ESF #5 to the Logistics Section at the EOC. 
i. The Logistics Section Chief has several options available to fill the 
requirements: 
1. Fill the requirement from current resources. 
2. Fill the requirement by asking for State resources. 
3. Procure the goods or services through commercial sources. 
4. Once the source is identified, the resource is delivered to the 
location specified by the Logistics Section Chief. The Logistics 
Section Chief is responsible for all transfer of goods to jurisdiction 
control. This may occur at the following locations: 
a. Incident Command Post 
b. Logistical Base 
c. The staging area. 
d. Distribution point. 
e. Directly where the resource is needed. 
i. If the resource was filled from existing County or 
local jurisdiction resources, replenishment activities 
begin immediately to replace the resource upon the 
recommendation of the County Commissioners. 
Responsibilities 
The primary agency responsible for Logistical Management at the EOC is Cheyenne/Laramie 
County Emergency Management Agency.  The ESFs are responsible for supporting the EOC and 
the needs of Logistical Management. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
141 
 
 
Purpose 
The purpose of this annex is to outline the means, organization, and process by which we will 
provide appropriate information and instructions to the public and media during emergency 
situations.  This annex also provides for public education to be conducted in advance of 
emergency situations to reduce the likelihood that citizens will place themselves in hazardous 
situations that may require an emergency response. 
General 
Pursuant to the National Incident Management System (NIMS) operating principles and protocols, 
public information efforts should generally focus on specific event-related information.  This 
information will generally be of an instructional nature focusing on such things as warning, 
evacuation, and shelter.  Appendix 2 describes some basic emergency information needs. 
A special effort should be made to keep the public informed of the general progress of events.  
Reporting positive information regarding emergency response will help to reassure the community 
that the situation is under control.  Rumor control must be a major aspect of the informational 
program.  Public feedback should be used as a measure of the program’s effectiveness. 
Education efforts are to be directed toward increasing public awareness about potential hazards 
and how people should prepare for them.  All information and education efforts will rely heavily 
on the cooperation of every type of media organization. 
Information Dissemination 
In the initial stages of an emergency situation, the Laramie County Communications Center may 
have to take action on time-sensitive hazards.  Within the limits of the authority delegated to it, 
the Incident Commander will determine if a warning needs to be issued and disseminate it through 
the Public Information Officer (PIO).  Pre-scripted emergency messages have been prepared for 
likely hazards and are included in the “Warning” section of the Base Plan.  A list of these 
messages is provided in Appendix 5.  These pre-scripted messages may be used as written or 
tailored as needed for specific circumstances. 
As the Emergency Alert System (EAS) messages are limited to two minutes, EAS warning 
messages may have to be supplemented with Special News Advisories prepared by the PIO staff 
that contains amplifying emergency information.  Special News Advisories are generally 
disseminated to media outlets by fax.    Copies of the pre-scripted messages, which include 
warning messages and Special News Advisories, should be maintained on computers at the 
Office of Homeland Security and in the EOC so that they can be modified quickly. 
Broadcasters and cable companies must carry national security warnings and messages initiated 
by the President; they may broadcast alerts and messages initiated by state and local 
governments.  The Federal Communications Commission encourages licensees to broadcast 
local warning and instruction messages, but the final decision on broadcasting such messages 
rests with the broadcasters.  Broadcasters and cable operators will expect EAS to be used for 
life-threatening emergencies.   
 
 
Support Annex 3 - Public Affairs 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
142 
 
When the Incident Command System is activated for an emergency situation, the Incident 
Commander will normally warn the public in and around the incident site.  A designated PIO at 
the Incident Command Post (ICP), assisted by the County/City (depending on location of incident) 
PIO staff if necessary, will normally provide information on the emergency situation to the media 
if the EOC has not been activated. All information relayed to the media by the PIO will be approved 
by the IC and the EOC/CEO (depending on location of incident), regardless of the command 
structure – single or unified. 
Once the EOC has been activated for an emergency situation, the EOC Manager will normally 
determine the need for additional warning and instructions.  The PIO staff will disseminate Special 
News Advisories and other emergency public information materials to the media directly using its 
contact list. 
In the case of large-scale emergencies or disasters where there are substantial external 
responders from other jurisdictions and/or state or federal agencies and the response and 
recovery effort may continue for an extended period, a Joint Information Center (JIC) may be 
established.   The JIC, an element of the Joint Information System (JIS) developed to provide 
information to the public during an emergency, is a working facility where the emergency public 
efforts of all participating jurisdictions, agencies, volunteer organizations, and other responders 
can be coordinated to ensure consistency and accuracy. In federally declared incidents, a JIC will 
typically be set up as part of the Joint Field Office (JFO). 
The following means will be used to provide emergency information and instructions to the public, 
if available: 
 
EAS broadcasts by radio, television, and cable companies. 
 
CodeRed/IPAWS Emergency Notification.  
 
Special news broadcasts by radio, television, and cable companies. 
 
Local newspapers. 
 
Telephone warning/information system. 
 
Mobile police/sheriff units with public address systems. 
 
Recorded information on available information hotlines. 
 
The local government Internet site. 
 
Other 
Providing Emergency Information to Special Populations 
Special populations will be provided information on emergency situations and appropriate 
instructions by the following methods: 
 
Visually-impaired:  EAS messages and news advisories on radio, NOAA Weather Radio, 
or by door-to-door notification 
 
Hearing-impaired:  Captioned EAS messages and news advisories on television, print 
media 
 
Non-English Speakers:  Announcements in Spanish on radio, TV, or cable language 
newscasts, public address systems, door-to-door, etc…

Phases of Management  
1. Mitigation 
a. Conduct hazard awareness programs.  
b. Develop systems to enhance information dissemination during emergency 
situations. 
2. Preparedness 
a. Develop and distribute educational materials; conduct public education programs.  
b. In coordination with the IC/EOC, prepare pre-scripted warning and public 
instruction messages for known hazards.  
c. Brief local media on local warning systems and coordinate procedures for 
transmitting emergency information to media. 
d. Conduct public education on warning systems and the actions that should be taken 
for various types of warnings. 
e. Train public information staff. 
f. Brief local officials and emergency responders on working with the media.   
g. Maintain this annex. 
h. Identify suitable facilities for a Joint Information Center.  
3. Response 
a. Develop, obtain authorization, and release public information on the emergency 
situation.  
b. Conduct media monitoring to determine the need to clarify issues and distribute 
updated public instructions. 
c. Manage rumor control. 
d. Conduct news conferences and arrange interviews as needed.   
4. Recovery  
a. Provide public information relating to recovery process and programs. 
b. Compile record of events.  
c. Assess effectiveness of public information and education program.  
Organization and Assignment of Responsibilities 
General  
The overall responsibility for providing emergency information and instructions to the public rests 
with the CEO and/or IC/UC depending on the situation.  The CEO shall provide general guidance 
for Public Information (PI) programs and appoint a Public Information Officer (PIO) in connection 
with the IC/UC. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
144 
 
The PIO will manage and coordinate all emergency public information related activities and direct 
such staff as may be assigned or recruited to assist in those activities.  Trained public information 
specialists will staff PIO positions at the Incident Command Post (if requested) and in the EOC. 
Task Assignments 
1. The Chief Elected Official will: 
a. Appoint a Public Information Officer (PIO). 
b. Ensure that the jurisdiction has implemented and institutionalized processes and 
procedures to coordinate and integrate public information functions including the 
development of a public education program for emergency situations. 
c. Work with IC/UC to authorize release of all approved incident information to the 
media. 
d. Ensure that a Joint Information Center (JIC) is activated when warranted by the 
incident. 
2. The Public Information Officer (PIO) will: 
a. Represent and advise the IC on all public information matters relating to the 
management of the incident. 
b. Ensure the IC approves the release of all incident-related information. 
c. Coordinate and integrate public information functions across jurisdictions and 
functional agencies as required. 
d. Develop accurate and complete information on the incident for both internal and 
external consumption. 
e. Coordinate the overall emergency public information efforts of local government. 
f. Serve as the official County/City representative in the JIC. 
g. Conduct public education programs as an ongoing activity.  
h. Develop and disseminate public information materials and maintain a stock of 
materials for emergency use based on hazards likely to confront the jurisdiction.  
Such materials should include: 
i. General materials dealing with the nature of hazards and basic protective 
actions to take in the event of an emergency, including shelter-in-place and 
evacuation. 
ii. Hazard specific instructions on “where to go and what to do” in an 
emergency. 
iii. Information on how emergency warnings are disseminated and the 
meaning of warning signals. 
i. 
Develop methods (i.e., newspaper supplements, prepared TV/radio scripts for 
broadcast stations) for distribution of information materials to the public, to include 
materials for non-English speaking groups, if appropriate.  
j. 
In cooperation with the CEO, coordinate with broadcasters (radio and television 
stations and cable television companies) to develop procedures for local 
government to disseminate warning messages and emergency information 
through the broadcast media.  
k. Authenticate sources of information, verify for accuracy, and obtain authorization 
before issuing news releases. 
l. 
Provide authorized news releases to the media while keeping the CEO, Mayors 
and Commissioners informed of message content.  
m. Monitor media coverage of emergency operations for accuracy of reports and 
issue corrections where necessary. 
n. Take action to control rumors. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
145 
 
o. Brief potential Incident Commanders, department heads and key staff, and the 
EOC staff on basic public information needs, working with the media, and media 
access during emergency operations.   
p. Maintain a media briefing area [in/in the vicinity] of the EOC. 
q. Periodically brief the media on local warning systems and warning procedures. 
r. Maintain a Media Contact Roster.  See Appendix 1 for a sample. 
s. Compile printed and photographic documentation of the emergency/disaster.  
t. Develop public information emergency checklists for known hazards.  See 
Appendix 6. 
u. Anticipate and be prepared to handle unscheduled inquiries from the media and 
the public. 
v. Train a group of government employees and/or volunteers to staff PIO positions at 
the Incident Command Post, if requested, and in the EOC. 
3. All local government departments and agencies will: 
a. Refer media inquiries during emergency situations to the PIO. 
b. Assist the PIO in responding to requests for information from the public or the 
media. 
4. Media companies are expected to: 
a. Disseminate warning messages and special news advisories provided by local 
government to the public as rapidly as possible.  
b. Participate in periodic tests of the EAS and other warning systems. 
c. Provide coverage of emergency management activities.  
d. Work with PIO on public educational programs relating to emergencies.  
e. Check accuracy of information on emergency operations with the PIO. 
Direction and Control 
General  
The CEO has the overall responsibility for the emergency public information program for their 
jurisdiction, and shall provide general guidance for emergency-related public education and 
information activities, shall appoint a PIO, and in conjunction with the IC, approve all information 
released to the news media. 
The PIO shall direct all emergency public information activities, coordinating as necessary with 
other individuals, departments, and agencies performing other emergency functions. 
To the extent possible, the PIO shall release, upon approval, all information to the public and the 
media during emergency operations.  During emergency operations, departments and agencies 
shall refer media inquiries to the PIO. 
Line of Succession 
The line of succession for the Public Information Officer is: 
1. Affected jurisdiction designated PIO 
2. Laramie County Sheriff’s Department PIO 
3. Chief Elected Official 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
146 
 
Readiness Level 
1. Readiness Level 4 - Normal Conditions 
2. Readiness Level 3 - Increased Readiness 
a. Monitor the situation. 
b. Check and update Media Contact Roster. 
c. Alert media of the increased threat so they are aware of the situation and are 
prepared to disseminate warnings and public instructions if necessary. 
3. Readiness Level 2 - High Readiness 
a. Monitor the situation. 
b. Review pre-scripted warning messages and public instruction messages; draft 
updated versions or additional messages tailored for the impending threat. 
c. Alert personnel for possible emergency operations; identify personnel for 
increased staffing during primary vulnerability period. 
d. Determine requirements for additional pre-emergency public information and 
instructions and produce and disseminate those materials. 
e. Consider placing public information personnel on shifts to provide for increased 
situation monitoring and to conduct additional public information planning. 
4. Readiness Level 1 - Maximum Readiness  
a. Monitor the situation. 
b. Update warning messages as necessary. 
c. Update public information materials based on current threat and disseminate. 
d. Provide information to the media on local readiness activities. 
e. Place selected off-duty personnel on standby to increase staffing if necessary. 
f. Staff public information positions in the EOC or at the ICP when activated. 
PIO Duties 
1. Media Contact Roster 
a. The PIO shall maintain a contact roster for the media organizations that are 
involved in local emergency management programs.   
2. Records 
a. The PIO shall maintain a file of all news advisories and press releases issued 
during emergency operations. 
b. The PIO shall also compile and maintain copies of newspaper articles, videotapes 
of emergency operations and news broadcasts relating to an emergency, and 
other media materials distributed for use in post-incident analysis and future 
training activities.  
3. Training 
a. Members of the public information staff for whom public information is not their 
primary daily work should attend public information training, preferably training 
focusing on emergency public information activities.

Appendix 2 – Public Information Needs 
Background  
During emergency situations, it is important to provide the general public with adequate 
information on the situation as rapidly as possible to alleviate concerns and reduce the likelihood 
of panic or inappropriate actions.  The news media are the primary means of disseminating such 
information by providing up-to-date information quickly to a wide audience.  The information they 
provide reduces the time and manpower that local government would have to divert from 
response and recovery tasks to deal with (which could be an overwhelming number of inquiries 
from the public).  Every effort should be made to cooperate with the news media in providing 
information and in recognition of the rights of the news media to perform their proper function. 
Information Needs  
The following types of information shall be provided to the public as soon as possible in as much 
detail as possible.  
1. What Happened 
a. Nature of incident or emergency  
b. Location 
c. Time of occurrence 
d. Situation resolved or response on-going 
e. Cause (Until an investigation has determined the cause with reasonable certainty, 
it is not advisable to speculate.)  
2. Current Response Actions 
a. What actions have been or are being taken to protect public health and safety and 
public and private property? 
3. Known Damages 
a. Homes 
b. Businesses 
c. Government buildings 
d. Infrastructure – roads, bridges, parks, etc. 
4. Casualties  
a. Number dead and apparent cause 
b. Number of injured and nature/severity of injuries and where they are being treated 
c. Number missing and circumstances 
d. General identification of casualties – age, sex, situation (employee, homeowner, 
responder, etc.) 
e. Names of casualties – only released after next of kin have been notified 
5. Evacuations 
a. Areas and facilities evacuated 
b. Approximate number of evacuees 
6. Shelter & Mass Care 
a. Shelters open – name and location 
b. Approximate number of persons being housed in shelters 
c. Mass feeding site or other mass care facilities in operation – name, location, and 
number of persons being served.  

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
151 
 
7. Status of Utilities  
a. Electric service 
b. Telephone system 
c. Water system 
d. Sewer system 
e. Natural gas distribution 
8. Road and Facility Closures  
9. Organizations Responding 
a. Local government 
b. State agencies 
c. Federal agencies 
d. Volunteer groups  
10. Means of contacting evacuees  
11. Areas to which access is restricted and the reason(s) for such restriction  
12. For ongoing emergency situations, planned response activities  
13. In the recovery phase: 
a. Disaster assistance programs available 
b. How to apply for disaster assistance 
Collection and Dissemination of Information  
Information shall be collected and disseminated as soon as possible by the appropriate personnel.   
All incident related information must be approved by the IC prior to dissemination. The PIO is 
responsible for collection of information from the Incident Commander, the EOC staff, and other 
sources and agencies.  The PIO staff is responsible for preparation of news releases, for the 
dissemination of information directly to the news media, and, where appropriate, for making 
arrangements for announcements directly to the public via radio and/or television hookups.  
Where an Incident Command Post has been established and a qualified public information staff 
member is at the scene, that individual may provide information directly to the media if the EOC 
is not activated.  If no qualified public information staff member is present at the scene, the 
Incident Commander or a member of his staff should pass situation information to the Public 
Information Officer for release to the media.  Where an Incident Command Post has been 
established and the EOC has been activated, information from the incident scene will normally be 
passed to the Public Information Officer at the EOC. The Public Information Officer will utilize 
reports from the scene and other available pertinent information to brief the media and prepare 
news advisories for release to the media. 
The Shelter and Mass Care Officer is responsible for collecting information on shelter and mass 
care activities and providing that information to the PIO.  The Energy and Utilities representative 
in the EOC is responsible for obtaining information on the status of utilities and providing it to the 
PIO.  Law Enforcement and Public Works/Engineering are responsible for obtaining information 
on road closures and facility closures and providing it to the PIO. 
Hospitals are responsible for dissemination of information concerning casualties and deaths.   
They generally have policies restricting the release of detailed information without permission of 
patients or their families.  The information that they choose to release will normally be 
disseminated directly to the news media.  The PIO should request that the EOC be provided 
copies of any information released to the media. 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
152 
 
Appendix 3 – Working with the Media 
What to do when working with the media: 
1. Identify your spokesperson beforehand. 
2. Have a number the media know to call when they need information. 
3. Make certain the person answering the phones knows to whom to direct media calls. 
4. Get all the information you can from those in charge before you talk with the media. 
5. Write out the answers to these questions for your use:  
a. What happened?     
b. When did it happen?  
c. Where did it happen? 
d. Why did this happen? 
e. Who's responsible, involved, injured? 
f. How many were hurt or killed?  What are their names/ages/addresses? 
g. Can I shoot video/take photos?  How close can I get? 
h. Who can I talk to? 
i. 
What is your agency doing about it? 
When you talk with the media: 
 
Tell the truth and if related to the incident, ensure the IC has approved the information. 
 
Be courteous and don't play favorites.  
 
Avoid "off the record" remarks. 
 
Never say anything you would not want to see printed or broadcast. 
 
Stay on top of the interview by listening to the reporter's questions. 
 
Don't accept the reporter's definitions of what happened. 
 
Pause, think; ask for more time if you need it. 
 
Respond only to the question you've been asked.  Don't speculate. 
 
Stick to the core message 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
153 
Appendix 4 – Media Access & Identification 
Media Access 
In recognition of the public’s right to know as much information as possible about a disaster, local 
response agencies will cooperate with legitimate news media representatives and provide equal 
access to information and, within the limits of safety and other response needs, access to incident 
scene to various news organizations.  News media representatives are required to cooperate with 
response personnel as directed for safety and efficient operation. 
The Incident Commander or his designated representative will allow media such access to the 
incident scene as is consistent with safety and does not disrupt critical operations.  
The CLCEMA, in coordination with the PIO, shall establish rules for media access to the EOC.   
When the EOC is activated, representatives of news media may be provided access to those 
areas of the EOC designated by CLCEMA.  As a general rule, press briefings will not be 
conducted in the EOC because they can disrupt on-going EOC operations; briefings will normally 
be conducted in the press area.  Photo shoots and interviews may be conducted in the EOC, but 
these should be scheduled so as to minimize disruption. 
Hospitals establish their own rules of access for news media representatives and these may vary 
for individual circumstances.  For emergency situations where there have been substantial 
casualties, it may be desirable for hospitals to provide a pressroom or other designated area with 
access to telephones for the use of news media representatives. 
When incident scenes are on private property, the property owner may establish and enforce 
policies with regard to access by the media and other persons who are not emergency 
responders. 
Media Identification 
Representatives of news media will be considered to have satisfactory identification if they have: 
 
A media company identification card with photo that identifies them as a media 
representative, unless there is reason to believe that the identification is not genuine. 
 
Show a press identification card.
