Tab A:  Public Information Checklist for Flooding 
Note:  This public information checklist is designed for slowly developing floods.  For a 
fast-breaking flood situation, it may not be feasible to conduct some of the readiness 
activities listed. 
 
Pre-Emergency Phase 
 
1. Conduct public education and distribute preparedness materials highlighting local flood risk 
areas, precautionary actions, and protective actions. 
 
2. In coordination with the EMA/EOC, maintain a set of pre-scripted warning and public 
instructions messages ready for use.  See Annex A to the Base Plan. 
 
3. Coordinate with school authorities/PIOs on policies/procedures for announcing school closures. 
 
4. Review local Hazard Analysis and Base Plan to identify potential flood risk areas and evacuation 
routes. 
 
5. Coordinate with Animal Control, Animal Shelter, and other organizations to determine 
availability of facilities for evacuated pets and large animals. 
 
6. Coordinate with PIOs from local response agencies and volunteer groups and develop an 
effective PIO-to-PIO communication system. 
 
 
Readiness Phase 
 
1. Ensure PIO receives current information on flood watches & warnings.  
 
2. Coordinate with the Shelter and Mass Care Officer to determine likely shelter sites. 
 
3. Coordinate with Law Enforcement to determine planned/likely evacuation routes. 
 
4. In coordination with EMA, update precautionary action and evacuation message(s).  
 
5. Develop maps of likely evacuation areas and evacuation routes that can be provided to the 
media. 
 
6. Disseminate property protection and evacuation preparedness information to public through 
media. 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
156 
 
Emergency Response Phase 
 
1. Provide evacuation area and evacuation route maps to media. 
 
2. Release evacuation recommendation through warning system. 
 
3. Release information on how transportation will be provided for those who lack it. 
 
4. Release public instructions on securing property, evacuation routes, and what to take with you. 
 
5. Release information to media on shelter and mass care facilities available. 
 
6. Release information to media on where persons needing assistance should call. 
 
7. Release special instructions for those evacuating pets. 
 
8. Release information on curfews and travel restrictions in effect within evacuation areas. 
 
9. Release information on disaster welfare inquiry procedures. 
 
10. Advise the public not to return to the evacuation area until told to do so.  
 
11. Inform media of emergency response actions and organizations participating. 
 
 
Post-Emergency Phase 
 
1. Coordinate with Law Enforcement to obtain information on routes for return of evacuees and areas 
where reentry is restricted due to damage. 
 
2. Coordinate with CEO to obtain and release damage assessments to media, updating as additional 
information becomes available. 
 
3. Release information to media on return of evacuees and preferred reentry routes, if any. 
 
4. Release information to media on access controls for damaged areas, if any. 
 
5. Provide public information on safety precautions for entering damaged buildings and the need to 
document damage and contact insurance companies. 
 
6. Release information on disaster relief/recovery programs and facilities. 
 
7. Release information to media on termination of shelter operations 
 
8. Release information on debris removal activities. 
 
9. Release information on volunteer assistance for home cleanup and repair.

Warning - Shelter-in-Place 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency has issued the following warning 
for those who live, work, or are visiting (location) 
 
 
 
 
 
. 
There has been an accidental release of hazardous material that is affecting a portion of the local 
area. People in the following area must take protective measures: [Describe area boundaries] 
If you are located in this area, do the following immediately in order to protect yourself: 
 
Go inside your home, workplace, or the nearest building that appears to be reasonably air 
tight and stay there. Take your pets with you. 
 
Close all doors, windows, and any fireplace dampers. 
 
Turn off any heating or cooling system that draws in air from the outside. 
 
Keep your radio/TV on and tuned to a local station to receive emergency announcements 
and instructions 
 
Gather items that you may need to take with you if you are advised to evacuate. 
People traveling in vehicles should seek shelter in the nearest air tight structure. If a suitable 
structure is not immediately available, travelers should roll up car windows, close air vents, and 
turn off the heater or air conditioner until they reach a suitable building. 
If shelter is not immediately available, keep a handkerchief, towel, or damp cloth snugly over your 
nose and mouth until you get indoors. 
(If school is in session) 
Students at the following school(s) are taking shelter at their schools: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


resolved and they are advised it is safe to do so.

 
Parents should not attempt to pick up students from schools that have been evacuated. 
 
Local officials will provide information on where to pick up school children as soon as it is 
available. 
If you know of any neighbors or co-workers with hearing or language problems or special needs, 
please advise them of this message. 
Please do not call 911 or local emergency officials for information. Stay tuned to this station for 
additional information.

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
168 
 
 
Purpose 
The ability of Laramie County to perform a rapid situation assessment accurately and within the 
first few hours after an incident is critical to providing an adequate response to life-threatening 
situations and imminent hazards that may impact our County, and our cities. Rapid Assessment 
(RA) will allow government officials the ability to prioritize response activities, determine available 
resources, allocate resources, and request mutual aid and State and Federal assistance. 
Scope 
The scope of this annex is applicable to all County agencies and political subdivisions that may 
be involved in an incident.  The purpose of RA is not to estimate the dollar value of the damage 
or the fine details. It is, rather, to assess the nature, magnitude and scope of the event so that the 
decision makers can assign the appropriate priorities to their response, utilizing the available 
resources most effectively, and requesting outside resources of the most appropriate types that 
are most needed. 
To accomplish this important purpose, it is necessary to get information that is geared to disclose 
the type of damage that has occurred, where it has occurred, what resources are realistically 
available, and the transportation limitations and capabilities that will affect the response. 
Specifically, what roads are blocked, where, and by what? What utilities are functional and what 
utilities need to be shut down to protect life or property. Where is the largest number of victims 
trapped, in need of medical care, or in need of shelter? What significant buildings are damaged, 
and which may be available for sheltering. 
Concept of Operations 
The County’s RA plan is designed to be used in a major event with massive damage. Under these 
circumstances, it must be anticipated that normal operations of the County and its usual priorities 
will be suspended in order to do the best for the most citizens in as little time as possible. 
Any major event can be expected to seriously disrupt, if not totally curtail, telephone 
communications. Cellular service is dependent upon the landline telephone system in some 
measure. Additionally, cellular towers, and particularly interconnection microwave equipment, are 
subject to physical damage. Moreover, cellular service is at least as prone to overloading as is 
the basic landline service. 
Although FEMA and other federal agencies will deploy people to begin damage assessment, it 
remains important for the local government and responders to have an accurate view of the extent 
of the incident.  
Rapid assessment teams must be formed and deployed quickly to provide a picture of the event. 
Government workers, first responders and subject matter experts can provide the most accurate 
description of the event.  
In a large scale event all government services can be impacted. By performing a rapid 
assessment, loss of life, damage to infrastructure and services can be assessed and informed 
decisions can be made as to the need of mutual aid, state assistance and federal assistance.  
Support Annex 4 - Rapid Assessment 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
169 
Responsibilities 
The basic concept of Rapid Damage Assessment (RA) is to help determine a fairly accurate 
overview of the full extent and nature of the situation in order that the most beneficial and effective 
decisions can be made with regard to event priorities, deployment of available resources, and 
requesting of additional outside resources. 
Every employee and every citizen has a role in RA. Each must first assess his or her personal 
situation and take whatever steps may be immediately necessary for their personal safety and 
that of those around them. In terms of the formal RA, the fire and police departments, together 
with the public services and public utilities departments, play the lead roles, with critical input from 
Community Resources (which include businesses, organizations, and volunteers). 
There are several important uses of the information gathered in RA. Initially, the on-scene Incident 
Commander (IC) uses the information to make initial planning and strategy decisions. Once the 
EOC has been effectively activated and coordination and policy staff have gathered, they use the 
information gathered from the RA process to assess the situation, make policy determinations, 
and formulate effective and realistic goals. At all levels, relevant accurate information is essential 
for effective decision making. 
Coordinator Responsibilities 
The RA Coordinator is assigned by the Emergency Manager or CEO to complete the following 
assessments (Checklists are available in the Rapid Assessment Appendix): 
 
Life Safety Operations 
 
Status of Lifelines 
 
Status of Facilities 
 
Status of Imminent Hazards 
 
Descriptions of Major Problems by Sectors 
 
Resource Utilization and Requests for Assistance 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
170 
Appendix A – Rapid Assessment Intelligence Categories 
Rapid Assessment Intelligence Categories 
The following is a list of categories for which rapid assessment information should be gathered: 
1. Life Safety Operations (Search & Rescue, Deaths, Injuries, Evacuation) 
a.  
Number of people potentially affected by location 
b.  
Number of people dead 
c.  
Number of people injured 
d.  
Rough estimates of displaced persons 
e.  
Collapsed buildings requiring search and rescue

Evacuation concerns (i.e. food, water, shelter) 
2. Status of Lifelines (Transportation System, Communication System, Infrastructure 
System, Energy System)

Railroads

Airports

Bridges and tunnels 
vii.  
Roadways (state, county, local)

Communication System

Local phone systems

iii.  
Cellular phone system 
iv.  
Cable television 
v.  
Radio

Other Systems

ii.  
Electric 
iii.  
Water

Government Complex(es)

EOC

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
180 
 
Purpose 
The Volunteer and Donations Management Support Annex describes the framework through 
which the EOC coordinates with other agencies and other jurisdictions, the private sector, 
volunteer organizations, and non-governmental organizations to ensure unsolicited donated 
goods and unaffiliated volunteers are effectively utilized during an Incident of Significance or 
emergency. 
Scope 
This support annex provides guidance on the jurisdictions’ role in volunteer and donations 
management in an Incident of Significance. Any reference to donated goods and services in this 
annex refers to unsolicited goods and unaffiliated volunteer services. This guidance applies to all 
agencies with direct and indirect volunteer and/or donations responsibilities under the ERP. 
Policies 
The role of a government is always in support of the disaster area within its jurisdiction. Laramie 
County and all jurisdictions within, in coordination with ESF #6 coordinator(s) are in charge of 
volunteer and donations management.  Laramie County has primary responsibility for the 
management of unsolicited goods and unaffiliated volunteers and will look principally to those 
voluntary organizations with established volunteer and donations management structures already 
in place to receive appropriate volunteers and donated goods. 
Necessary response activities for the management of unsolicited goods and unaffiliated 
volunteers are closely coordinated with County officials, the EOC, appropriate ESFs, and 
voluntary agencies.  Laramie County will encourage cash donations to recognized nonprofit 
voluntary organizations with disaster experience, and can set up an account for Laramie County 
Disaster Assistance. 
Laramie County encourages individuals interested in volunteering their personal services to 
affiliate with a recognized nonprofit voluntary organization to facilitate their involvement in disaster 
relief activities. 
All activities, functions, and services are provided without regard to economic status or racial, 
religious, political, ethnic, or other affiliation.  All activities, functions, and services are also 
provided in accordance with existing State and County and municipal statutes, rules, and 
regulations.  Full use of existing voluntary organization volunteer and donations management 
resources is encouraged before the assistance of the Federal or State governments is sought. 
Concept of Operations 
Volunteer and donations management response activities are often necessary during a disaster 
and hence require rapid coordination to mitigate potential volunteer and donations coordination 
problems. 
 
 
Support Annex 5 - Volunteer and Donations Management  

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
181 
Donated Goods Management Function 
1. Management of unsolicited donated goods and unaffiliated volunteers involves a 
cooperative effort by County and local jurisdictions, EOC, ESFs, voluntary and community-
based organizations, the business sector and the media. 
2. Laramie County, the affected jurisdiction, in conjunction with their voluntary organization 
partners, and ESFs, are in charge of developing donations management plans and 
managing the flow of donated goods during disaster response and recovery operations. 
3. Federal and State support may include: 
a. Enhanced voluntary agency coordination. 
b. Assistance in establishing a Donations Coordination Team and a Donation 
Coordination Center; 
c. Technical and managerial support. 
d. A national network of information and contacts to assist donations specialists in 
the field. 
e. Communications support as necessary. 
Volunteer Management Function 
1. Management of unaffiliated volunteers requires a cooperative effort by Federal, State, and 
local governments, voluntary and community based organizations, faith groups, the 
private sector, and the media. The EOC, ESF #6, in close partnership with voluntary 
organizations, are in charge of developing plans that address the management of 
unaffiliated volunteers during disaster response and recovery. 
2. Federal support provided may include: 
a. Enhanced voluntary agency coordination; 
b. Technical and managerial support; 
c. A national network of information and contacts to assist volunteer management 
specialists working in the field and 
d. Communications support as necessary. 
Responsibilities 
1. Volunteer and Donations Management Coordinator 
a. Makes early contact with the key Volunteer & Donations Organizations within the 
County to assess Donations and Volunteer Management. 
b. Provides donations program guidance, a Donations Coordinator, and other 
assistance as appropriate, based on the County’s citizens need for assistance. 
c. Provides support and assistance to the field, especially regarding large corporate 
offers, or politically sensitive collection drives. 
d. Coordinates international offers of assistance with the appropriate Federal 
agencies. 
e. Provides training in the preparedness period in Volunteer and Donations 
Management in providing technical assistance in the disaster response and 
recovery periods. 
f. Provides legal forms for Volunteers and Donations Management activities. 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
182 
g. Establishing a Volunteer & Donations Coordination Team to: 
i. Set up a Volunteer & Donation Coordination Center, including a volunteer 
and donations hotline. 
ii. Ensures the appropriate donations receiving and distribution facilities are 
established and operating effectively. 
iii. Coordinates with the Joint Information Center (JIC) staff on public service 
announcements, press releases, and other media-related support 
iv. Coordinates with the EOC, community relations, County and State 
Agencies and/or other agencies as necessary. 
v. Prepares input for situation reports, briefings to the EOC and CEO. 
h. Keeps Volunteer Waiver Records, Volunteer Training Records, and Time Records 
for all volunteers. 
2. Voluntary Organizations Active in Disaster: 
a. Assist in providing information about donations and volunteer management 
operations of member agencies.  
 
 
 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
183 
 
Introduction 
The Planning Annex identifies specific plans within the ERP that may be utilized and followed 
during and after an emergency or a disaster. These plans describe, in specific detail, the 
operational systems, coordination, logistics, and response protocols specific to those specialties 
and incident specific events in accordance with the Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency 
Response Plan. All plans should be based on the Incident Command structure described in the 
Base Plan.  
These annexes are designed to be the major support system for the EOC. These planning 
annexes play an important part in the needs of the affected jurisdiction to provide logistics, 
coordination, documentation, and help provide additional support to the incident. Each plan is 
kept within its responsible agency’s location and shared with agencies responsible with assisting 
in the implementation of the plan and available within the EOC for emergency use.  The following 
pages summarize the supporting plans to this Emergency Response Plan but are not kept within 
this document due to size.  Copies of the plans can be found at each responsible agency’s office 
and at the EOC. 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Snow Plan 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Sheltering Plan 
 
Laramie County Tactical Interoperable Communications Plan (TICP) 
 
Laramie County Cyber Security Plan 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Wild Fire Protection Plan 
 
Wyoming Region 7 Hazard Mitigation Plan 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Debris Management Plan 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Distribution Management Plan 
 
Cheyenne – Laramie County Health Department All Hazards Emergency Operations Plan 
 
Laramie County Coroner – Fatalities Management Operations Plan 
 
Wildland Fire Management Annual Operating Plan 
 
BOPU Dams and Reservoirs Emergency Action Plan 
 
 
 
Cheyenne/Laramie County ERP – Planning Annexes 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
184 
 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
Primary Location of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency, EOC, 
Elected Officials, Supporting Agencies 
This annex outlines the response by local government to severe winter weather conditions 
affecting the operations of government offices. The plan outlines late openings/delayed start, early 
closure and full closures of government offices. The plan also provides safety information about 
winter weather hazards and information you may need to survive severe winter weather. This plan 
affects the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County. 
This plan prioritizes safety. It can be implemented by both public and private organizations. The 
plan is a coordinated process between agencies to assess threatening weather and allow 
employees and citizens to determine the safest response to inclement weather. 
 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
Primary Location of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency, EOC, 
American Red Cross 
The Cheyenne/Laramie County Sheltering Plan is a co-written plan based on the response to 
sheltering needs within Laramie County under ESF #6, Mass Care Emergency Assistance, 
Temporary House, and Human Services.  The purpose of this plan is to utilize pre-determined 
sheltering locations based on the needs of the community at the time of the event.  The American 
Red Cross and CLCEMA work together to determine which location is best for the event and 
resources, to include volunteers, needed for sheltering.    
 
 
Planning Annex 1 - Cheyenne/Laramie County Snow Plan 
Planning Annex 2 - Cheyenne/Laramie County Sheltering Plan 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
185 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Combined Communications Center, 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
This document established a Tactical Interoperable Communications (TIC) Plan for Laramie 
County, WY.  The TIC Plan is intended to document the interoperable communications resources 
that are available within the jurisdiction, who controls each resource, and what rules of use or 
operational procedures exist for the activation and deactivation of each resource. 
This TIC Plan is intended to apply to the jurisdiction, as defined above. Specifically, the plan is 
intended for the first responder disciplines that would respond to the scene of an emergency, as 
well as other disciplines that would need to be coordinated with during the response. These 
include but are not limited to: 
 
Law Enforcement 
 
Fire Service 
 
Emergency Medical Services 
 
HazMat 
 
Public Safety Communications 
 
Emergency Management 
 
Public Works 
 
Public Health 
 
Government Administration 
 
Transportation 
 
Health Care 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Laramie County Information Technology 
Primary Location of Plan:  Laramie County Information Technology, Cheyenne/Laramie County 
Emergency Management Agency 
Laramie County has a large internal network comprised of many different departments.  All 
departments have different network requirements.  It is the job of I.T. to maintain the security of 
all the systems that the many departments use. Network security includes Authorization, Internal 
Networks, Cloud Based Services, WIFI, Data Encryption, Application Updates, Web Browsing, 
Remote Access, Vendor access. 
Laramie County’s IT Systems are available to users and connected processors that have been 
granted access to those resources. Authorization mechanisms are used to allow, deny or limit 
access to IT Systems. Authorization is: based on a unique user authentication as; granted for 
data access with read, write, update rights, etc., depending on the authority given to the user or 
the connected processor; or granted on the basis of classification regarding the confidentiality, 
availability and integrity of the data to be accessed. Authorization is required to use IT Systems 
such as systems (computers), applications (e.g. word processing), functions (e.g. create account), 
data (e.g. contents of a memo).   
Planning Annex 3 - Laramie County Tactical Interoperable 
Communications Plan (TICP)

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
186 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management 
Agency, Laramie County Fire Warden 
Primary Location of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency, 
Laramie County Fire Warden, Laramie County Fire Districts 
This document has been prepared to assist Laramie County residents, local government, 
emergency responders and land management agencies in responding to national and state 
wildfire management direction and in achieving the following goals: 
1. Protect the lives of residents and emergency personnel. 
2. Protect property and critical infrastructure in the Wildland Urban Interface. 
3. Protect key environmental values and quality of life. 
To those ends, the objectives of the CWPP are to: 
1. Provide a comprehensive review of the existing community risks of wildfire using best 
available data and current methodology to validate identified risk areas. 
2. Recognize the mitigative actions that have been taken or are underway in the communities 
in the Plan area. 
3. Achieve collaboration by bringing together diverse local, state and federal interests to 
identify essential community values; discuss mutual concerns for public safety, community 
sustainability; and natural resource protection. 
4. Share information from the community assessments with private landowners, public land 
managers, the Fire Warden and Fire Protection Districts, and Public Safety Officers to 
reduce damage to the values identified as at risk. 
5. Foster coordination between all the stakeholders in the Plan area. 
6. Assist in obtaining eligibility for funding and other assistance from State and Federal 
agencies for wildfire mitigation, appropriate equipment and training. 
 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency, 
Goshen County Emergency Management, Platte County Emergency Management, Wyoming 
Office of Homeland Security 
Primary Location of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency, 
Goshen County Emergency Management, Platte County Emergency Management, Wyoming 
Office of Homeland Security, City of Cheyenne and Laramie County Planning & Development 
offices 
The State of Wyoming’s Office of Homeland Security (WOHS) coordinated with Wyoming Region 
7 Counties to create a cross-regional Hazard Mitigation Plan (HMP). The purpose of the HMP is 
to help plan for and implement hazard mitigation projects and activities, with the goal of reducing 
or eliminating losses from future hazard events. The counties of Laramie, Goshen, and Platte, 
and with their respective jurisdictions and communities, led these efforts to conduct local hazard 
assessment and analysis. Their efforts have demonstrated a commitment and perseverance to 
mitigating future risk. This plan will be used as a tool for Region 7 communities to guide resiliency 
and help decision makers’ direct mitigation activities.  This HMP was also developed in order to 
meet requirements set forth by the Federal Emergency Management Agency (FEMA), so that 
communities are eligible for various disaster assistance funding opportunities. This plan will be 
updated and maintained in order to address the natural hazards addressed in the Plan. 
Planning Annex 5 - Cheyenne/Laramie Wild Fire Protection Plan 
Planning Annex 6 - Wyoming Region 7 Hazard Mitigation Plan 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
187 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
Primary Location of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency, 
Laramie County Public Works, City of Cheyenne Public Works 
THIS PLAN IS CURRENTLY UNDER DEVELOPMENT 
The Debris Management Plan recognize natural and human-caused disasters which have the 
potential to create debris that can disrupt the quality of life for citizens, and complicate disaster 
response and recovery following such disasters.  Planning for such disasters can lessen the 
impact on the community, economy, and the environment.  Therefore, Laramie County is currently 
developing this plan to facilitate a rapid response and recovery to debris causing incidents.   
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
Primary Location of Plan:  Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management 
THIS PLAN IS CURRENTLY UNDER DEVELOPMENT 
A Distribution Management Plan establishes strategies, functional plans, and tactical guidance 
for logistical response operations. This plan covers staging sites and operations, logistical support 
including services and personnel, information management, transportation of resources to point 
of need, commodity points of distribution (C-PODs), inventory management, resource sourcing, 
and demobilization. A Distribution Management Plan includes sections with information on the 
following seven components: Requirement Defining, Resource Ordering, Distribution Methods, 
Inventory Management, Transportation, Staging, and Demobilization.   
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne - Laramie County Health Department 
Primary Location of Plan:  Cheyenne - Laramie County Health Department, Laramie County 
EOC, Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
The purpose of the All Hazards Emergency Operations Plan (EOP) is to describe the response 
of the Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Department to a public health emergency.  This includes 
the preparation and planning before an event and the efforts during an event to minimize, as much 
as possible, the impact on the public health and safety of Laramie County.  A public health 
emergency, in the context of the EOP, is defined as, but is not limited to:  a natural disaster or a 
disease that threatens a Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Department activity and/or facility, 
that threatens the health of Laramie County citizens, or that exhausts the capabilities of a 
community’s medical infrastructure; the appearance of an unusual or of a previously controlled or 
eradicated infectious agent or biological toxin; OR a man-made disaster (may be intentional or 
accidental). 
 
 
 
Planning Annex 7 - Cheyenne/Laramie County Debris Management 
Planning Annex 8 - Cheyenne/Laramie County Distribution 
Planning Annex 9 - Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Department 
All Hazards Emergency Operations Plan 

Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Operations Plan 
 
 
188 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Laramie County Coroner 
Primary Location of Plan:  Laramie County Coroner’s Office, Laramie County EOC, 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
The purpose of this plan is to coordinate the operational response of the jurisdictional members 
of Laramie County, Wyoming in the event of a mass fatalities incident. This Fatalities Management 
Operational Response Plan will address the mortuary surge capacity issues and provide methods 
to respond to and mitigate such issues. 
The focus of this plan will be to identify methods through which the Coroner within Laramie County 
may obtain support assets to accomplish the goals of identifying the deceased and arranging 
proper final disposition. This plan should not be considered all inclusive, but rather presents major 
categories of service response that must be adapted to the nature of disasters ranging from 
natural, man-made or technological. 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Wyoming State Forestry Division 
Primary Location of Plan:  Laramie County Fire Warden, Laramie County Fire Districts, 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Emergency Management Agency 
This operating plan is a Sub-Geographic Area Annual Operating Plan (AOP) meant to address 
issues affecting cooperation, interagency working relationships and protocols, financial 
arrangements, and joint activities across the State of Wyoming.  The AOP details operating 
procedures applicable across the State and those applicable to specific districts.  Procedures 
applicable to specific districts are included within the attachments tied to the plan and District 
Specific Procedures, which are incorporated herein by this reference.  Signatories to the AOP 
agree to procedures applicable across the State and to those applicable to their specific district.   
 
Responsible Agency of Plan:  Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities 
Primary Location of Plan:  Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities, Cheyenne/Laramie County 
Emergency Management Agency 
The possibility of dam failure affecting Laramie County is extremely small however, if one were to 
fail, it would be a catastrophic event. Documented dam failures have demonstrated the short 
period of time it takes for a dam to breach. The large and uncontrolled release of water resulting 
from dam failure would be very destructive. Other dam safety issues could arise and lead to 
catastrophic events, too. The purpose of this Emergency Action Plan (EAP) is to establish a 
procedure to notify authorities of potential danger so that people may be smoothly and quickly 
moved to safety. This EAP pertains to the resulting water release that could occur from the 
possible dam failures and other dam safety issues that might occur. It describes the actions 
necessary to accomplish the warning and evacuation of the areas that would be inundated. It 
establishes a course of action for certain key personnel so that they can guide the actions of 
others. 
Planning Annex 10 - Laramie County Coroner Fatalities Operational 
Response Plan 
Planning Annex 11 - Wildland Fire Management Annual Operating 
Planning Annex 12 - Board of Public Utilities Dams and Reservoirs 
Emergency Actions Plans

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND THE CITY 
CLERK TO SIGN A FINAL PLAT FOR CAREY COTTAGES, CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING (LOCATED SOUTHEAST 
OF THE INTERSECTION OF GROVE DRIVE AND CHEYENNE 
STREET)." 
 
 
WHEREAS, J Fish, LLC has subdivided said land in accordance with the statutes in such 
cases made and provided; 
 
 
WHEREAS, J Fish, LLC the owner of the above-described land, has caused a subdivision 
plat of said land to be made, acknowledged, and certified, particularly describing the lots, blocks, 
easements, and rights-of-way; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the above-described subdivision plat has been presented to the City of 
Cheyenne Planning Commission for consideration; the Planning Commission has recommended 
that said plat be approved by the Governing Body; and the plat has been duly executed by the 
Development Office. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the subdivision described as Carey Cottages, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, be and the same hereby is approved and confirmed as presented, and that the Mayor 
and the City Clerk be and are hereby authorized, empowered, and directed to execute said plat, 
when Community Facility Fees have been paid in full. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor

PUDC-24-32 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Carey Cottages Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing Body 
with staff recommended conditions and acknowledgements. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
x 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
x 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
x 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for roughly 0.15 acres of land. The subject property 
is vacant. The proposed plat would subdivide the parcel into four townhouse lots. These lots would meet the 
requirements to be considered AD1 Lots, which are permitted in the MR Medium-density Residential zone.  
 
Townhome (“attached”) lots are not allowed front access if the lot is less than 36’ wide. The plat does not 
provide an access easement or alley for access to the central lots. These lots would need relief from UDC 
4.3.6.c to allow front access. Should the Planning Commission and the Governing Body find the development 
pattern acceptable and approve the plat, a subdivision standards waiver would be granted by Staff for access 
to Cheyenne Street. 
 
Although the plat as proposed meets all dimensional standards in the UDC for AD1 lot types, the specific 
product desired by the applicant would require setback relief. Should the Planning Commission and the 
Governing Body find the development pattern acceptable and approve the plat with the understanding of 
the product proposed, an administrative adjustment would be granted by Staff for reduced rear setback. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1)

PUDC-24-32 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land and surrounding area has 
been designated as Urban Residential. The Urban Residential designation encourages a range of 
more intensive residential uses, although the intent is not to change existing stable 
neighborhoods. The existing neighborhood has a few lots that have two units on a single lot. The 
proposed development fits within the range of the existing neighborhood, while providing a 
different housing option. 
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 7 of PlanCheyenne supports the use of existing infrastructure and timely provision 
of services (including emergency and education services). This development is proposed in an 
area which has largely existing infrastructure and limited expansion of infrastructure is 
necessary. 
  
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat is in an area with existing infrastructure and no changes to the street or utility 
network are proposed. The proposed plat would not change the existing conditions in a way 
which would adversely affect the ability of surrounding properties to redevelop. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. The acreage in the plat is small enough the minimum fee of $250 of Park 
Fees will be due at signing of the plat. 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$125 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$125  
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$250 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-32 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is occurring within an area of the community which has been previously developed. 
There is limited opportunity to modify the block layout or street network.  
 
Townhome (“attached”) lots are not allowed front access if the lot is less than 36’ wide. The plat 
does not provide an access easement or alley for access to the central lots. These lots would 
need relief from UDC 4.3.6.c to allow front access. 
 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space is required at 750sqft per unit or 8% of the gross development area, whichever 
is less. However, due to the nature of this application, plat-level open space is not required. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. A utility easement is requested to be added along Cheyenne St. by BOPU.  
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any residential building on any 
of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the issuance 
of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage worksheet was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with drainage at 
the plat level for the project as presented.  
 
Traffic: 
A transportation worksheet was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with traffic at 
the plat level for the project as presented.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 

PUDC-24-32 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. The proposed lots 
would be considered AD1 Lot Types for townhouse style development. The lots being created 
are shallow lots which would only allow a property to have a 23’ deep dwelling unit, if the unit 
were to meet all setbacks (a 58’ deep lot with a 15’ front setback, 20’ rear setback). There are 
dwelling units in the City which are as little as 20’ deep; however that is not the product the 
applicant is proposing. The applicant has requested relief for the rear setback as their proposed 
product would encroach by 10-feet into the rear setback. Should the Planning Commission and 
Governing Body accept the reduction in the rear setback, Staff will grant the Administrative 
Adjustment with the approval of this plat. 
 
The Administrative Adjustment Review Criteria is attachment number 6. Also attached is the 
applicant’s requested adjustment. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
All proposed lots are buildable lots. The Plat does not propose any lots subject to this criterion. 
 
Review criterion not applicable. 
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development. While the 
lots proposed are smaller than all of the lots in the area, there are other lots that have multiple 
units on a single lot. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 

PUDC-24-32 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends minor 
corrections as conditions to the plat. These corrections should be resolved prior to the final plat 
being recorded.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections. 
2. Utility and easement correction. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant with conditions. 
 
As noted in criteria 6, the applicant has requested relief from the rear setback requirements for their 
proposed product. Staff would ask that it be taken into consideration when reviewing the plat.   
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 

PUDC-24-32 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following acknowledgements and conditions: 
1. Acknowledgements of relief: 
a. Access to a street for lots less than 36’ wide (Subdivision Standards Waiver) 
b. Reduction in a rear setback (Administrative Adjustment) 
2. Prior to recording: 
a. A 10-foot utility easement be added along Cheyenne Street. 
b. A Public access easement be added along Cheyenne Street for a 6-foot attached 
sidewalk. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat 
6. Administrative Adjustment Information

MEMO
To:
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council
From:
Athen Mores
Subject:
PUDC-24-30 | Sun Rise Hills North Subdivision, 3rd Filing – Final Plat
Date:
April 8, 2024
Background: 
The item for consideration is a Final Plat.  This is approved by Ordinance.   
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows:
x
Introduction and Referral  
04/08/2024 
x
Public Services Committee  
04/16/2024 
x

04/22/2024

05/13/2024 
Planning Commission Recommendation: 
The Planning Commission heard this item at their April 1, 2024 meeting and recommended the 
Governing Body approve this application unanimously.
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed. 
Attachments:
1.
Ordinance
2.
Planning Commission Staff Report
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND THE CITY 
CLERK TO SIGN A FINAL PLAT FOR SUN RISE HILLS NORTH 
SUBDIVISION, 3RD FILING, CITY OF CHEYENNE, LARAMIE 
COUNTY, WYOMING." 
 
 
WHEREAS, Taiga Homes LLC, has subdivided said land in accordance with the statutes 
in such cases made and provided; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Taiga Homes LLC, the owner of the above-described land, has caused a 
subdivision plat of said land to be made, acknowledged, and certified, particularly describing the 
lots, blocks, easements, and rights-of-way; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the above-described subdivision plat has been presented to the City of 
Cheyenne Planning Commission for consideration; the Planning Commission has recommended 
that said plat be approved by the Governing Body; and the plat has been duly executed by the 
Development Office. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the subdivision described as Sun Rise Hills North 
Subdivision, 3rd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming, be and the same hereby is approved and confirmed 
as presented, and that the Mayor and the City Clerk be and are hereby authorized, empowered, and 
directed to execute said plat, when Community Facility Fees have been paid in full. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
9:42 am, Apr 03 2024


PUDC-24-30
April 1, 2024
Planning and Development Office
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282  (Fax) 307-637-6366
CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
April 1, 2024
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-30 Sunrise Hills North Subdivision, 3rd Filing (Expedited Review)
REQUEST:
A final plat creating 12 lots that include 12 single family unit residential lots 
on 2.08 acres of land.
LOCATION:
West of intersection at Thomas Rd and Ridge Rd
APPLICANT:
AVI (Cody Bruso)
OWNER:
Pronghorn Construction LTD
ZONING:
MR Medium Density Residential
FUTURE LAND USE: Urban Residential
PREPARED BY:
Athen Mores, Planner
Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-30 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Sunrise Hills North Subdivision 3rd Filing Expedited Plat, advancing the Final Plat to 
the Governing Body with staff recommended exception. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
x 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
x 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
x 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for 2.08 acres of land. The subject property is 
vacant land, previously platted for single-family development on larger lots.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
The proposed project is to develop 12 singe family unit residential lots.  This development is in 
conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in PlanCheyenne. The property 
is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. The future land use map 
within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with residential uses.     
  
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2)

PUDC-24-30 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
 
The proposed plat divides the land into 12 individual parcels. Surrounding development largely 
consists of single-family residential development on a variety of lot sizes.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$700.00 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$700.00 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$1400.00 
 
Land being replatted can credit fees paid for a previous plat toward those due. Park fees 
have not changed since this land was last platted. No fees are due with this platting action.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee $1,100 per acre 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
The owner is requesting a waiver for the transportation network as the lot adjoins Thomas 
Rd to the south and Ridge Rd to the east.  The property is in the middle of an already 
developed area of Cheyenne that has existing connections to other roads and no space for 
additional roads. A previous plat for this location received an exception previously to allow 
the larger than standard block at this location. This exception should be renewed with this 
platting action. 
 
Open Space (4.4): 
The owner is requesting a waiver for the requirement of an open space narrative due to 
meeting criteria 4.4.2.b.3. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any commercial building or 
residential building on any of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees 
are payable at the issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

PUDC-24-30 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage study was submitted and reviewed.  There are no concerns with the study provided. 
 
Traffic: 
A traffic study was submitted and reviewed. There are no concerns with the study provided.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
This plat is proposing 12 buildable lots. They will be used for single family unit residential 
developments at the proposed size.  All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this 
zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
There are no non-buildable lots for the proposed project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable. 
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat is adjacent to a large undeveloped parcel to the north. There is potential for this plat to 
create a road to the north to allow development of the property to the north. Surrounding 
development largely consists of single-family residential development on a variety of lot sizes. 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing development. The plat proposes 12 
buildable lots, and has access points to the south at Thomas Rd and to the east at Ridge Rd.  

PUDC-24-30 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
No remainder lots for the proposed project.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-30 
 
 
April 1, 2024 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, all comments received regarding the following issues. 
1. Utility easement dimensions  
2. Title block name and changes 
3. Exact coordinate location 
4. Pronghorn Construction Signatures and Notaries regarding affidavit  
5.  Drainage easement width. 
 
The applicant has received and is coordinating updates. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application with an exception.   
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing Body, with 
the following exception: 
1. The following exception is to be granted: exception to maximum block size. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat
